## 2026-11-24 · People review (main session)
| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PI-R-1 | Someone who was added and later opens the bill's link counts as having joined: `join_bill` sets `added = false` (review R6) | Joining makes you active | Stay "added" until you claim | Open |
| PI-R-2 | The claim path takes the bill lock first, so a claim made at the same moment as a remove fails cleanly instead of deadlocking (review R1) | Fix in the DB now | Map deadlocks to a retry in the app | Open |
| PI-R-3 | Direct claim inserts and deletes through the Data API stay allowed for now. The app only reads `claims` directly (`lib/bills.ts:524`) and writes through `set_claim`, but `set_claim` is security invoker and relies on those grants. A direct delete racing `update_bill` can still deadlock. | Keep; listed in before-production | Make `set_claim` security definer, then revoke INSERT/DELETE on `claims` from `authenticated` | Kept (user, 2026-11-24): accepted risk; assume nobody writes claims directly through the API |
| PI-R-4 | An added person who opens the link logs a `joined` event, so Activity shows "Sam joined" after "Hana added Sam" | Log it | Skip the event for added→joined | Open |

## 2026-11-24 · Claimable discounts (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-24-claimable-discounts.md](specs/2026-11-24-claimable-discounts.md))
Decided by the PM under the user's delegation ("pick the best simple option, keep building, log every important decision").

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CD-1 | Default for each discount | Split (today's behavior) | Claim by default | Nothing changes unless the host flips it; most coupons are for the whole table | Open |
| CD-2 | The control | One switch per discount row in the bill form, "Claim like an item", with a caption when it's on | A Split/Claim segmented row; one setting per bill | The user asked for a small toggle; each discount row already has a segmented control (before/after subtotal) | Open |
| CD-3 | How a claimable discount is stored | A `line_items` row with `kind = 'discount'` and `applies_to` | Separate `bill_discounts` + `discount_claims` tables | Reuses claims, `set_claim`, `set_item_split` (sharing by parts), realtime, Activity, "complete" and the membership rules, with no new write paths or RLS | Open |
| CD-4 | Sign of the stored amount | Negative `total_cents` | Positive, with `kind` giving the sign | "Total minus all rows" stays the extras formula, and anything that sums rows stays right | Open |
| CD-5 | Sharing a discount between people | Parts 1–20 in the same sheet as items, no percentage input | A percentage field | The request says "exactly like items"; parts already allow any split | Open |
| CD-6 | A claimed discount bigger than someone's items | Allowed: their share can go negative, and the settle screen shows "… owes you, settle outside the app" (existing Q6 card) | Cap at their items and share the rest; block the claim | The discount was theirs, so being owed money is the fair result. Sums stay exact with no extra rule. A cap's leftover, shared out, can land back on the same person and push them below zero again. **Needs your look: changes what someone can owe** | Open |
| CD-7 | Does a claimed discount lower someone's base for proportional tax and tip? | No: weights are the items people claimed, before discounts | Weights net of the discount, floored at 0 | Weights never go negative (no new edge cases); tips are usually figured before discounts; simpler to explain | Open |
| CD-8 | Who shares tax and tip in even mode | People with at least one claimed **item**; claiming only a discount doesn't count | Any claimed row | ADR 0014 says "claimed an item"; someone who only claims a comp shouldn't pay a share of the tip | Open |
| CD-9 | A claimable discount nobody has claimed | Counts as unclaimed: settling is blocked, even-mode totals stay hidden, and the Unclaimed line shows the negative amount | Falls back to Split while unclaimed | Same rule as items, and the DB already enforces it (`bill_is_complete` counts every row), so app and DB can't disagree; a fallback would make shares jump when someone claims it | Open |
| CD-10 | "N items left" wording | ~~Unclaimed discounts count as items; copy unchanged~~ **Amended 2026-11-24 (spec Amendment A1, review R1):** only items left keeps today's copy; only discounts left says "1 discount left to claim" / "every discount is claimed" / "all discounts are claimed"; both left says "2 items and 1 discount left to claim" / "everything is claimed" | A separate "1 discount left" | Original: fewer strings. Amended by the main session: with every item claimed, "1 item left" sent people looking for an item that doesn't exist | Amended (A1) |
| CD-11 | Host edits after claims | Amount, label or before/after: claims kept. Claim → Split, $0 or delete: claims removed after a confirm. Split → Claim: a new unclaimed row. A row can't change kind in place (the DB rejects it) | Block edits once claimed | Matches how item price changes and deletions work today | Open |
| CD-12 | A $0.00 claimable discount | Not stored as a row | Store it | A $0 row would block settling until someone claims nothing | Open |
| CD-13 | Activity | Reuse `item_added` / `item_removed` / `item_changed` with `discount: true`; the sentences say "the discount Police comp". Claims read "Sam claimed Police comp". A before/after change alone isn't logged | New event kinds | No constraint change; the reader already handles these kinds; before/after moves nobody's money | Open |
| CD-14 | Order in the claim list and on reload | Discounts after all items under a "Discounts" caption. On reload, split discounts (still two sums) come first, then the claimable ones | Store split discounts row by row to keep exact order | Storing split discounts one by one is a bigger change; out of scope | Open |
| CD-15 | Who may claim a discount | Any participant, like items | The host assigns it | Same rule as items; Activity shows who claimed it | Open |
| CD-16 | Even mode before everything is claimed | "Your items" shows items only; discounts appear with the totals | Show items minus claimed discounts early | Keeps the existing "totals hidden" rule in one place | Open |
| CD-17 | Migration timestamps | `20261124094657` (columns, validation, create_bill) and `20261124094658` (update_bill) | — | Next after `20261124094656`; split so each task stays under ~300 lines | Open |
| CD-18 | App/DB money rules | Only the totals check (C2) and "complete" (C3) exist on both sides, as the same rule with shared boundary tests. Shares stay app-only, as before | Compute shares in SQL | No new app/DB exception (unlike K6 / PI-M-1) | Open |
| CD-T3-1 | A row id that appears twice with two different kinds (`item` and `discount`) in `computeShares` input | Throw `RangeError`, whatever the order | Keep the first kind seen; sum anyway | Ids are unique in the DB, so this can't come from real data; "first seen" would make the result depend on input order, which the split math promises it never does (Task 3 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T3-2 | A `kind` value outside `'item' \| 'discount'` reaching `computeShares` at runtime (untyped data) | Throw `RangeError` | Treat it as an item | Same "fail loudly on bad money input" rule as non-integer cents (lesson 2026-11-21); `fetchBill` already rejects unknown kinds (Task 4) (Task 3 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T3-3 | Where criterion 24's `settlePlan` checks (E2, K-D1) live | In `lib/split.test.ts` | Add them to `lib/settle.test.ts` | Task 3 owns only `split.ts` / `split.test.ts`; `settlePlan` itself needs no change (Task 3 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T3-4 | Test helper `allValuesIntegers` in `lib/split.test.ts` | Also checks `discountCents` and `unclaimedDiscountCents` | Leave it as it was (criterion 25 allows only `discountCents` literal edits) | Test-only and stricter; no existing assertion's value changed (Task 3 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T1-1 | A row with `"kind": null` sent to `create_bill` / `update_bill` | Rejected (`22023 invalid_bill`) | Treat null like an absent `kind` (item) | C1 says "if present it must be the JSON string"; an explicit null is more likely a client bug than an item (Task 1 supabase-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T1-2 | A group position holding rows of two kinds | Rejected in `validate_bill_rows` (explicit "one kind per group" check) | Rely on the group-size check alone | The group-size check already rejects it today (a discount is a group of 1); the explicit check keeps it rejected if that rule ever loosens (Task 1 supabase-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T1-3 | Item rows that do send `"kind": "item"` and/or `"applies_to": null` | Accepted, same as rows without those keys | Reject the redundant keys | C1 allows them ("absent or JSON null"); the app sends neither, so this only matters for other callers (Task 1 supabase-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T1-4 | `update_bill` between Task 1 and Task 2 | Unchanged: it validates with the new rules but inserts new rows without `kind`, so a new discount row fails `line_items_kind_shape` (23514) and nothing is written | Ship both migrations as one | The spec splits the work; the window is closed by `20261124094658` and no app code sends discount rows yet (Task 1 supabase-engineer) | Closed by `20261124094658` (Task 2) |
| CD-T4-1 | `SavedRow.kind` / `applies_to` (and so `BillRow`'s) in the TypeScript type | Optional (absent = item, null), while `parseRow` always sets both | Required on `BillRow` | Making them required breaks hand-built rows in tests outside this task (`lib/membership.test.ts`, `lib/billView.test.ts`, screen fixtures); same "absent means item" rule as `SplitItem.kind` in C4 (Task 4 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T4-2 | A fetched `line_items` row with no `kind` / `applies_to` keys at all | Read as an item (the item rules, cents ≥ 0, still apply). A present `kind: null`, an unknown kind, or a kind/sign/`applies_to` mismatch is `network` (criterion 34) | Treat a missing key as malformed | The select always names both columns, so only test fakes lack them; 7 screen-test files outside this task seed rows without them and would otherwise all fail (Task 4 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T4-3 | A claimed claimable discount that is both switched to Split **and** set to $0.00 in one edit | Listed in `removedClaimedIds` ("Saving deletes …"), not `splitClaimedIds` | "Sharing … with everyone" | Nothing is shared at $0, so "deletes" is the accurate sentence (Task 4 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T4-4 | The 500-row limit (`too_many_units`) | Counts claimable discount rows along with item units; split discounts don't count | Count item units only | `validate_bill_rows` limits all of `p_rows`, so the form must block what the DB would reject (Task 4 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T4-5 | `saveConfirmCopy`'s new `splitClaimedIds` argument | Optional, and its sentences come after the "Lowering…" and "Saving deletes…" ones, one per discount in bill order | Required | Existing callers (the edit screen, until Task 6) keep compiling and read exactly as before (Task 4 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T4-6 | `rowArg` given an item row that carries `kind: 'item'` or a stray `applies_to` | Strips both: item rows are sent byte-for-byte as before | Pass `kind: 'item'` through | C1: "the app sends item rows without `kind` or `applies_to`" (Task 4 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T2-1 | Where `update_bill` checks a kept id's kind | Right after the "id belongs to this bill" check, before the merge (R4) check, any event and any write; `kind` is never written on update, only `applies_to` (for discount rows) | Let the row's kind overwrite the stored one | A row can't change kind in place (CD-11), and checking before the events keeps "rejected edits change nothing" true, event count included (Task 2 supabase-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T5-1 | `breakdownText`'s third argument (`discountCents`) given a positive or non-integer value | Throw `RangeError` | Ignore it / format whatever it is | It is always ≤ 0 from `computeShares`; same "fail loudly on bad money input" rule as CD-T3-2 (Task 5 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T5-2 | `breakdownText` when a person has claimed discounts **and** negative extras (split discounts outweigh tax) | "Items $20.00 − claimed discounts $1.00 − discounts $5.00": items, then claimed discounts, then extras | Merge both into one "− discounts" figure | Keeps each figure equal to one `PersonShare` field, so the line adds up to the person's total (Task 5 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T5-3 | `splitInputFor` / `splitPreview` on item rows | Pass `kind` only for discount rows; item rows stay `{ id, totalCents }` | Always pass `kind: 'item'` | Item input is byte-for-byte what it was, and the existing `toEqual` on the split input passes unedited (Task 5 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T5-4 | Activity: `item_added` with `discount: true` and `qty` other than 1 | Malformed (null, skipped) | Show " ×2" like items | A discount row is always `group_size = 1` (C1), so another quantity can't come from `update_bill` (Task 5 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T5-5 | Activity: the sign of `price_cents` on discount events | Not checked (any integer cents, as for items) | Require negative cents when `discount: true` | Items don't check sign either, and `formatCents` prints the sign as stored, so a wrong sign can only show a wrong number, never unsafe text (Task 5 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T5-6 | Activity: a renamed discount | "Hana renamed the discount Comp to Police comp"; a combined change reads "Hana changed the discount Police comp: name …, price …" | Only prefix the price sentence | Spec rule: discount events read like the item sentence with "the discount " before the name (Task 5 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T6-1 | The switch control | React Native's built-in `Switch` on the right of a "Claim like an item" row; tapping the text flips it too. Only the switch is a screen-reader element and a keyboard tab stop | A custom pressable toggle; a third segment | Native look and feel on iOS, Android and web (an `<input role="switch">`) with no new code to maintain; the text tap gives a bigger target (Task 6 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T6-2 | Where the switch sits | Its own line under the before/after row, inside each discount row; the caption shows under it only when on | Beside the amount; in the before/after row | Rows are already full at phone width; a separate line keeps every target 44 pt and reads top to bottom (Task 6 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T6-3 | Deleting a claimed discount that the host has just switched to Split (not saved yet) | Asks with the same delete question ("1 person claimed this. Delete it anyway?") | Delete at once because it's Split now | The saved row still has claims, and deleting it removes them; asking is consistent with how the save would have asked ("Sharing … with everyone") (Task 6 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T6-4 | Switch colors | Track: `border` off, `primary` on. Thumb: iOS native white; Android and web `surface`. All pairs ≥ 3:1 in light and dark (3.74–9.29) | Platform defaults | Web's default thumb is teal on our teal track; using tokens keeps dark mode right (Task 6 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T6-5 | The delete confirm button's screen-reader hint for a discount | "Removes this discount and its claims" | Reuse the item hint | The item hint would say "item" for a discount (Task 6 product-engineer) | Open |

## 2026-11-24 · Bill markers and in-app notices (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md](specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md))
Decided by the PM under the user's delegation ("don't stop for questions; pick the best option and log it"). The user's request: an in-app notice (not push) when you're added to a bill, plus markers on Home rows such as "haven't claimed" and "haven't looked yet". Changes PI-6.

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM-1 | What "New" means | Someone added you (`added = true`) and you haven't opened the bill or dismissed its notice. Never for the host or for people who joined with the link | (A) Any bill you haven't opened; (B) "changed since you last looked" (new events since your last visit) | People who joined with the link opened it to join, and the host created it, so "New" would only flash for them. (B) fires on every claim, which is noisy, and needs event comparison. The user asked for a simple first version | Open |
| BM-2 | Where "seen" is stored | A new owner-only table `bill_seen (bill_id, user_id, seen_at)`, removed with your participant row; no client privileges; read and written only through two definer functions | A `last_seen_at` column on `bill_participants` | Every co-participant can read every `bill_participants` column (table-level SELECT), so the host could read your "seen" time: a read receipt. Updating that table also fires its broadcast, so everyone's open bill screen would refetch whenever you opened it. **Stores new per-person usage data (when you open a bill), visible to nobody else: needs your look** | Open |
| BM-3 | When a bill counts as seen | The bill screen, once per visit, after it loads; also "Dismiss" on the notice | Mark it when the row is tapped on Home | Covers every way into the bill (Home, the notice, a typed web URL) | Open |
| BM-4 | What "Dismiss" does | Marks the listed bills as seen, so "New" goes too and "Nothing claimed yet" takes over | A separate per-user "dismissed at" time that keeps "New" on the rows | One piece of state, and the notice and the "New" pills always cover the same bills. After dismissing, the remaining nudge is the useful one (claim). Easy to split later | Open |
| BM-5 | When "Nothing claimed yet" shows | You aren't the host or the payer, you have no claims on any row, and the bill isn't complete (`private.bill_is_complete`) | Everyone with no claims; also on complete bills | The host and payer organize and may not have eaten. Once the bill is complete, settle says you owe or owe nothing, so the marker would contradict it. "Incomplete" is exactly the state where a missing claim blocks settling | Open |
| BM-6 | An unclaimed claimable discount | Counts as "not complete" for the marker | Count only item rows | One definition, the database's `bill_is_complete`, the same one settling uses (CD-9, CD-10). No app copy of the rule | Open |
| BM-7 | How many markers per row | One, in priority order: New, then Nothing claimed yet | Up to two side by side | Quieter rows. Opening the bill clears "New" and shows the next one | Open |
| BM-8 | Marker form and copy | A small text pill, "New" / "Nothing claimed yet", and the row's screen-reader label adds "New, you haven't opened it yet" / "You haven't claimed anything yet" | An unread dot; colour only | The words carry the meaning, so colour is never the only signal | Open |
| BM-9 | Notice form | One card on Home. One bill: "Hana added you to a bill", "Nov 20 · $44.80", Open / Dismiss. Two or more: "You were added to N bills", "They're marked New below.", Dismiss. Plus "New" on the rows | A dot only; one card per bill; a banner over the screen | A card is noticeable but never interrupts (the user's nudge preference: cards on Home, never modals). One card keeps Home calm when several adds arrive | Open |
| BM-10 | Where the card sits | First on Home, above "Split a bill" | Below "Split a bill", like the Venmo card | It's time-sensitive, and the Venmo prompt is not | Open |
| BM-11 | How the card appears while the app is open | Home refetches on focus (as today) and when the app comes back to the foreground, also on web through tab visibility. No Realtime, no polling | (A) A per-user Realtime channel; (B) polling every N seconds | No new Realtime topic, policy or trigger, and nothing runs in the background. **Gives up:** someone looking at Home while being added sees nothing until they open something or switch apps. (A) is the upgrade if testers miss adds | Open |
| BM-12 | Home order | By when the bill entered your list (your `joined_at`), newest first | By bill date (today's order, PI-6) | An added bill shows at the top next to the card, and an older bill you're added to isn't cut off by the 20-bill limit. For hosts and link joiners the order barely changes. Changes PI-6's "Home keeps ordering by bill date" | Open |
| BM-13 | How Home gets its data | One definer function `my_bills()` returns each bill's summary plus `added`, `seen`, `claimed_any`, `complete` (replaces the `bills` select) | Extra selects plus computing in the app | One round trip and one consistent snapshot. Your seen state is readable only through it. "Complete" stays one rule in SQL | Open |
| BM-14 | The "block" gap | Not built. "Leave this bill" on the bill screen stays the defence; the notice has no Leave button | "Leave" on the card; a block list checked in `add_participant` | One home per action. A block needs its own design (who sees what, undo). Notices make repeated unwanted adds more visible, so the before-production item is updated | Open |
| BM-15 | Bills you were added to before this ships | No backfill: they show "New" until opened | Mark every existing row as seen | We don't know whether you saw them, and only local dev data exists | Open |
| BM-16 | `mark_bill_seen` on a bill you're not on | `42501 not_allowed`, the same for "no such bill" | A silent no-op | Same as `leave_bill`; tells the caller nothing RLS hides | Open |
| BM-17 | `mark_bill_seen` failing on the bill screen | Silent: no error, no retry | Show an error | "Seen" is a convenience; it must never get in the way of claiming. The next visit tries again | Open |
| BM-18 | Activity and live updates for "seen" | None: no event, no broadcast | Log "Sam opened the bill" | Read state is private. A broadcast would also let the host infer when you opened it | Open |
| CD-T8-1 | How Task 8 ran the app's TypeScript data layer against the live DB | `node --require <preload> --import tsx` from the scratchpad. A preload swaps `lib/supabase.ts` (React Native client) for a stand-in that forwards to the current throwaway user's client, and stubs `react-native` (`Linking`/`Platform`/`AppState`). Every other `lib/` module is the real code. Realtime clients get the app's `ws` package as the transport (Node 20 has no native WebSocket) | Retype the queries in a `.mjs` (the skill's default); run through Jest | Exercises the real `createBill` / `updateBill` / `fetchBill` / `toBillArgs` / `draftFromSaved` / `freshSaveCheck` / `settlePlan` / `describeEvent` code paths, not copies (Task 8 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T8-2 | Order of events from one `update_bill` call when they're read back | The check compares kinds as a set. All events from one transaction share `created_at`, and `fetchActivity` breaks ties by `id desc` (random uuids), so Activity can show `amounts_changed` above or below `item_removed`. This isn't new (any multi-event edit since M2) and isn't a C1–C5 mismatch | Ask for an insertion-order column (DB change) | The order of the two lines in one edit carries no money meaning. It's listed so the main session can decide whether Activity should keep insertion order (Task 8 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T8-3 | An edit that changes only a kept discount's `applies_to` (with the subtotal moved so C2 holds) | Logs nothing at all, not even `amounts_changed`: `amounts_changed` doesn't track `subtotal_cents`, and the discount sums and total didn't move. Matches the spec ("logs nothing; it moves nobody's money") | — | Observed live (EDIT-b); recorded so nobody expects an `amounts_changed` there (Task 8 implementer) | Open |
| CD-T7-1 | How the "Discounts" section looks on the claim screen | Its own card under a small muted "Discounts" header (screen-reader header), after the items card; no header when the bill has no claimable discounts | One card with a caption row inside it | Two cards read as two groups at a glance and keep the item list unchanged; the header costs one line only on bills that have one (Task 7 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T7-2 | Claim toggle hints on a discount row | "Takes this discount off your share" / "Puts this discount back for someone to claim" (items keep "Adds this item…" / "Removes this item…") | Reuse the item hints | "Adds this item to your share" would tell a VoiceOver user the opposite of what a discount does. Needed one optional `kind` prop on `ClaimItemRow` (Task 7 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T7-3 | Item sheet wording for a discount | "Claim this discount to change how it's split." and the reset hint "Everyone on this discount gets the same amount off"; title, preview, steppers and buttons unchanged | Keep "item" | Same sheet, one noun swapped; nothing new on screen (Task 7 product-engineer) | Open |
| CD-T7-4 | Claimed discounts in the summary's per-person line | Shown only with the totals (proportional, or even mode once complete), via `breakdownText(items, extras, discount)`; the line appears when extras **or** the claimed discount is non-zero (E2 reads "Items $5.00 − claimed discounts $10.00") | Show it while totals are hidden | CD-16 keeps "totals hidden" in one place (Task 7 product-engineer) | Open |

## 2026-11-24 · Bill markers, implementer decisions (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md](specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md), Tasks 2–3)

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM-T2-1 | How the notice's "Open" label is exported (K2 says "as exported constants", but it contains the host's name) | `addedNoticeOpenLabel(bill)` returns "Open <Host>'s bill" using the same trimmed-name / "A friend" rule as the title; the hint and both Dismiss labels and hints are plain constants (`ADDED_NOTICE_*`) | A constant template string the screen fills in; an extra `openLabel` field on the `'one'` notice | The title and the label can't disagree about the name, and `addedNotice` keeps the exact `{ kind, bill, title, detail }` shape the spec gives (Task 2 implementer) | Open |
| BM-T2-2 | Where the notice's name rule lives | A private `noticeHost` in `lib/homeMarkers.ts`, the same rule as `billListLabel` (trim; null or blank → "A friend") | Export `cleanName` from `lib/billView.ts` | `lib/billView.ts` is being edited by another session and was off-limits. Worth folding into one exported helper later (Task 2 implementer) | Open |
| BM-T2-3 | A host whose display name is literally "You" | The notice reads "You added you to a bill" (names are shown as written) | Special-case it | Same as `billListLabel` today ("You's bill"); a display name is the person's own choice, and the viewer's own bills never produce a notice (H11). Tested so the behavior is deliberate (Task 2 implementer) | Open |
| BM-T3-1 | How strictly `listMyBills` reads `my_bills()` beyond D2's table | Also `'network'` for: data that isn't an array, a list item that isn't an object, a non-string `host_name`. Columns outside D2 are dropped, never passed on. `created_at` must now be a parseable timestamp (the old `bills` select accepted any string) | Accept and coerce | Same "malformed is network, never coerced" rule as every other read in `lib/bills.ts`; D2 asks for a parseable `created_at` (Task 3 implementer) | Open |
| BM-T3-2 | "Name the columns in every query" for an rpc | `supabase.rpc('my_bills', {})` with no `.select(...)`: the function's result columns are fixed by its `returns table (...)`, and no table is read, so there is no `*` expansion. The parser reads only the 11 named D2 columns | Chain `.select('bill_id, host_id, …')` onto the rpc | Criterion 21 specifies `rpc('my_bills', {})`. The column-grant `*` problem (lessons 2026-11-24) applies to table reads, not to a definer function's declared result. If the reviewer wants the explicit column list, it's a one-line change plus a fake that supports `.select` on rpc (Task 3 implementer) | Open |
| BM-T3-3 | `lib/billView.test.ts` (another session's area) | Added the six D2 fields to the one `BillSummary` literal at the "home list label" block; no assertion touched | Retype `billListLabel`'s parameter as a `Pick<...>` | The spec (Task 3) names this exact edit, and changing the parameter type would mean editing `lib/billView.ts`, which was off-limits (Task 3 implementer) | Open |

## 2026-11-24 · Bill markers, screen decisions (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md](specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md), Tasks 5 and 6)

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM-T5-1 | Where the visible "Open" text lives | New constant `ADDED_NOTICE_OPEN_TEXT = 'Open'` in `lib/homeMarkers.ts`, with a unit assertion | A literal in `components/AddedNotice.tsx` | Criterion 39: every word on the card comes from `lib/homeMarkers.ts` | Open |
| BM-T5-2 | How long a dismissed bill stays hidden from the card | Home remembers a dismissed bill by its id plus your `joined_at` (`dismissKey`), until a load shows that bill isn't "new" any more (seen, gone from the list, or re-added with a new `joined_at`); then it's forgotten. While the server still says "new" for the same id and `joined_at` (write in flight or failed) it stays hidden for as long as Home is mounted. Pure helpers `dismissKey` and `stillDismissed` in `lib/homeMarkers.ts`. Changed after review (R2): it was keyed by id alone | Remember every dismissed id until Home unmounts (D5's literal wording); key by id alone | Home is mounted for the whole signed-in session. Keyed by id alone, a bill whose dismiss failed and that was removed and re-added before Home refreshed would stay hidden; a re-add gives a new `joined_at`, so it now gets its card back (D7). The cases D5 protects (an early refetch, a failed write) behave exactly as specified | Open |
| BM-T5-3 | The reload after Dismiss | Always once, after every `mark_bill_seen` has settled, even when all of them failed; skipped if Home has unmounted | Reload only on success | D5 says "after every call has settled". The reload also picks up anything else that changed. Skipping after unmount avoids work nobody sees | Open |
| BM-T5-4 | Double taps | Dismiss: a `useRef` set of dismissed ids; a second tap before the card is gone sends nothing. Open: no guard, same as the bill rows today | Guard Open too | Dismiss writes; Open only navigates, and the rows already behave this way. Guarding Open alone would make two controls for the same bill behave differently | Open |
| BM-T5-5 | Pill look and place | Its own line under the row's text, left-aligned, small semibold caption in a rounded tint. "New": `primary` on `primarySurface` (4.84:1 light, 7.80:1 dark). "Nothing claimed yet": `textMuted` on `surfaceMuted` (6.79:1, 7.04:1) | Pill to the right of the label; the same colour for both | Beside the label, "Nothing claimed yet" squeezes the label into more lines on a narrow phone. "New" is the time-sensitive one, so it gets the accent; the claim nudge stays quiet (the user wants calm screens) | Open |
| BM-T5-6 | Card look | A plain `Card` like the Venmo card: title as a heading, detail in muted tabular figures, "Open" as a secondary (outline) button and "Dismiss" as a ghost button. Not announced as a live region | A tinted card; Open as the primary button; announce it | "Scan receipt" stays the one primary action on Home. It's first in reading order, so screen readers reach it first; announcing it on every focus refresh would be noisy. Whether it's noticeable enough is the user's call in U1 step 7 | Open |
| BM-T6-1 | Where "mark seen once" lives | Inline in the bill screen: a `useRef` flag and one effect that runs when the load first becomes ready | A `lib/useMarkSeen.ts` hook (the spec allows either) | Six lines with one caller; a hook would add a file without making it clearer | Open |
| BM-T6-2 | A first load that fails, then "Try again" succeeds | Marks it seen then (the first ready load), once | Only if the very first load is ready | D6 says "after the first load that comes back ready"; the person did open the bill | Open |
| BM-T6-3 | How the old whole-rpc assertions were narrowed | To "every rpc call except `mark_bill_seen`" (helper `rpcCallsExceptSeen` in each file), not to the one function name | Filter to the function each test meant (e.g. only `set_claim`) | Keeps them exactly as strict as before: "Cancel calls nothing" still fails if anything other than the seen mark is sent | Open |

**User checks (need a person):** the spec's U1 (web, two accounts: card on tab return, Open, Dismiss with 2 bills, and whether the card feels noticeable but not pushy), U2 (VoiceOver reads "…, New, you haven't opened it yet", "Open A's bill", "Dismiss" with hints), U3 (iOS simulator: card appears after Cmd-Shift-H and reopening) and U4 (light and dark mode: pills readable, never colour alone). The screens weren't looked at in a browser or simulator in this task.

## 2026-11-24 · Bill markers, database decisions (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md](specs/2026-11-24-bill-markers-and-notices.md), Task 1; migration 20261124144925_bill_seen.sql)

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BM-D-1 | File names | Migration `20261124144925_bill_seen.sql` (the main session's name); test `supabase/tests/database/bill_markers.test.sql` (the spec's name) | Spec's `<ts>_bill_markers.sql`; test named `bill_seen.test.sql` | The main session assigns migration names. The test keeps the spec's name because the spec and the before-production item point to it | Open |
| BM-D-2 | `service_role` on `bill_seen` | Keeps Supabase's default privileges; only `anon`, `authenticated` and `public` are revoked (as D1 says) | Revoke from `service_role` too | It isn't an audit log: nothing can be forged that matters, and the service key is admin-only, never in the app. An admin may need to clear seen state by hand | Open |
| BM-D-3 | `seen_at` on a repeat call | `greatest(stored, now())` | Plain `now()` | `now()` is the transaction's start time, so two overlapping calls could move it backwards. `greatest` keeps criterion 4 ("≥ the first value") true under concurrency, and it's still `now()` in every normal case | Open |
| BM-D-4 | A participant removed between the unlocked check and the lock | `mark_bill_seen` checks participation again after `lock_bill_key_share`, and maps a foreign-key failure (23503, an account-deletion cascade racing the call) to `42501 not_allowed` | Rely on the FK alone; surface 23503 | The app maps only `42501` for this function (D2/D3), so every refusal must look the same. The same pattern as `leave_bill` (R4) | Open |
| BM-D-5 | Distinct times in the pgTAP ordering tests | The test sets `joined_at` / `created_at` directly (as postgres) on fixture rows only | Sleep between calls; `clock_timestamp()` defaults | `now()` is constant inside the one test transaction, so every fixture would tie. Changing defaults just for tests would alter behaviour | Open |
| BM-D-6 | Criterion 13's "three fixtures" | Three claim states of one bill (P), each compared with `private.bill_is_complete` | Three separate bills | Same coverage (all claimed; one unit of a two-unit item unclaimed; items claimed but the claimable discount unclaimed) with fewer fixtures | Open |
| CD-A1-1 | Where the unclaimed discount-row count comes from (R1) | `sharesComplete`, `displayShares` and `settlePlan`'s `blocked` gain `unclaimedDiscountRows` (of `unclaimedRows`); copy helpers take it as an optional second count (default 0) | Count `kind === 'discount'` in the screens | Components stay free of arithmetic; the default keeps every old call and string unchanged (Amendment A1 implementer) | Open |
| CD-A1-2 | The person's screen-reader label while totals are hidden (R1) | Uses the same "all discounts / everything is claimed" wording as the visible pending line | Leave it saying "all items" | Same mismatch as R1, one line away (Amendment A1 implementer) | Open |
| CD-A1-3 | Scanned text over the DB limits (R2) | `fromParsed` clips item names and discount labels to 100 characters and notes to 200, counting code points like Postgres's `char_length`, trimmed | Show a field error instead | The form had no length errors for names; clipping a scanned line loses nothing the user typed. Notes have the same 200 limit and the same failure, so they're clipped too (Amendment A1 implementer) | Open |
| CD-A1-4 | `RowKind` (R4) | Defined once in `lib/split.ts`; `lib/billPayload.ts` imports and re-exports it | Define it in billPayload | split.ts is the lower-level module that billPayload already imports from (Amendment A1 implementer) | Open |

## 2026-11-25 · Venmo note with item names (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-25-venmo-note-item-names.md](specs/2026-11-25-venmo-note-item-names.md))
User request: "add splitit + the items name in the note". U1–U5 were decided by the user (relayed by the main session, 2026-11-25); VN-1 … VN-11 were decided by the PM under the user's delegation.

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| U1 | Shared items in the note | Included | Only items claimed alone | Decided by the user | Kept (user) |
| U2 | Claimable discounts in the note | Left out | Shown as names | Decided by the user | Kept (user) |
| U3 | How many names | At most 2, then "+ N more" | All names up to a length cap | Decided by the user | Kept (user) |
| U4 | Limit hit before 2 names fit | As many whole names as fit, then "+ N more" | Clip the second name | Decided by the user | Kept (user) |
| U5 | One very long name | Clipped with "…" so the note fits, "+ N more" after it if others remain | Drop it | Decided by the user | Kept (user) |
| VN-1 | Format | `SplitIt: A, B + N more` (colon after SplitIt, ", " between names, " + N more" as in the user's example) | "SplitIt - A/B"; "SplitIt (A, B)" | Matches the user's example; reads naturally in a Venmo feed | Open |
| VN-2 | Whose items | Only the paying person's own claimed item rows, in bill order | Everything on the bill | It's what that person is paying for; other people's orders never go into someone else's payment | Open |
| VN-3 | Quantities | Names only, no "2×" | "2× Margarita" | With shared unit rows and custom parts a count can be misleading (you may own half of one of two margaritas); fewer characters for the 2-name limit | Open |
| VN-4 | Unit rows / repeated names | De-duplicated by exact cleaned name, first position kept | Show each unit; de-dup case-insensitively | One rule covers qty>1 unit rows and two lines with the same name; "+ N more" counts distinct names | Open |
| VN-5 | Length limit | 100 code points (starting value) | Venmo's documented 280 | Venmo's help center says notes are limited to 280 characters, but how it counts emoji and how a link-prefilled note is cut are unverified; with at most 2 names, 100 only matters for very long names | Open (verify in U-1) |
| VN-6 | Name cleanup and special characters | Collapse whitespace, trim, drop blanks, lone surrogates → U+FFFD, emoji kept, `encodeURIComponent` (spaces become %20, "+" becomes %2B) | Strip emoji/punctuation | Item names are typed or scanned by people; keeping them as written is least surprising. The lone-surrogate guard stops `encodeURIComponent` from throwing | Open (U-1 checks how Venmo shows them) |
| VN-7 | No item names | Plain "SplitIt" | "SplitIt: your share" | Same as today; nothing to list | Open |
| VN-8 | Privacy default | Item names on, behind a one-line code switch `VENMO_NOTE_INCLUDES_ITEMS` | Off by default; a per-person "show items in Venmo note" toggle | You asked for the names. Venmo payments can be public depending on each person's Venmo settings, so the note now reveals what someone ordered. This reverses M3's Q7 privacy reason ("never contains item names"). **Needs your look** | Open |
| VN-9 | Show the note on the Settle screen | No | A "Note: …" caption under the handle line | The user wants uncluttered screens; Venmo shows the note before paying | Open |
| VN-10 | Where the logic lives | `venmoNote` and `claimedItemNames` in `lib/paylinks.ts`; the screen passes `viewerItemNames` | Build the note in `lib/settleView.ts` | One builder for everything in the Venmo link; callers still can't pass a free-form note | Open |
| VN-11 | Task split | One implementer task (lib + 3-line wiring, no UI change) and one docs task | A separate product-engineer task for the screen | No visible change on the screen, so no design pass is needed | Open |
| VN-I-1 | How B20 (switch off) is tested | Exported `venmoNoteWith(names, includeItems)`; `venmoNote` calls it with `VENMO_NOTE_INCLUDES_ITEMS` | `jest.isolateModules` with a mocked constant | Plain function, no module mocking; the test also asserts the switch is on and `venmoNoteWith(…, true) === venmoNote(…)` | Open (implementer) |
| VN-I-2 | Room for a later Venmo "charge" link | Private `venmoLink(format, txn, input)` with `type VenmoTxn = 'pay'`; `venmoPayUrlForFormat` calls it with `'pay'` | Copy the builder per txn kind | The Venmo Request spec can widen `VenmoTxn` and add an exported wrapper without touching the note or validation; no charge feature added now | Open (implementer) |
| VN-I-3 | `VENMO_NOTE_MAX` | Kept at 100 | Raise toward 280 | No new evidence; U-1 is the check | Open (implementer) |
| VN-I-4 | Non-string entries at runtime | `venmoNote` skips them | Throw / coerce with `String()` | N2 says "never throws"; the type already forbids them, so this is only a guard | Open (implementer) |
| VN-I-5 | Screen test for criterion 8 (claimed "Police comp") | New test that adds the discount and Sam's claim of it, then presses Pay (label matched by regex since the amount changes) and checks the decoded note is `SplitIt: Salad, Fries`; the existing blocked-discount test near line 457 is left as is | Edit the existing blocked test | That test checks the blocked state, where there's no Pay button | Open (implementer) |

## 2026-11-25 · Venmo Request buttons (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-25-venmo-request-buttons.md](specs/2026-11-25-venmo-request-buttons.md))
User request: the payer can send Venmo requests to people who owe them (`txn=charge` link confirmed on iPhone). Decided by the PM under the main session's delegation. Builds on the Venmo note spec (VN-*); starts after its Task 1.

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VR-1 | Where the buttons live | A small ghost "Request" on each unpaid row of the payer's Everyone list, with a muted `@handle` next to it | A separate "Requests" card above the list; a Request button on the payer's summary card | The list already has one row per person with their amount and "Not yet"; putting the action there adds one line per unpaid row and no new section. The handle lets the payer spot a wrong username before Venmo opens (same reason as the Pay side's handle line, M3-UX-14) | Kept (user, 2026-11-25, U-A). Built 2026-11-25 (layout: VR-P1) |
| VR-2 | Who sees them | Only the payer (`viewerId === payerId`), on a ready plan | Also the host when the host isn't the payer | A Venmo request asks money for whoever is signed into Venmo on that phone; on the host's phone it would ask money for the host, who isn't owed it | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| VR-3 | Amount | Exactly the person's `owedCents` from `settlePlan`. No button for $0, negative or marked people | Let the payer edit the amount | The same number the person sees on their own card; no new money math, components do no arithmetic | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| VR-4 | "Request all" | Not built | One button that opens every request | Each link opens one request; browsers block every popup after the first in one tap, and on a phone each opens Venmo in turn. Per-row taps are as fast and predictable | Kept (user, 2026-11-25, U-A): no "Request all" |
| VR-5 | Marked "I paid", but the share grew since | No Request for the difference; today's "Marked $X · share now $Y ⚠" line stays | A "Request $2.00" on that row | Keeps one rule (unmarked owers only) and avoids a second amount on one row. Easy to add later with no DB change. **Money: needs your look** | Open. Built as picked 2026-11-25. Superseded by the settle-early spec (SE-4, SE-10 add difference buttons, including the payer's "Request $1.10"), which isn't built yet (checked 2026-11-25: no `settle_difference` in `lib/` or `supabase/migrations/`). **Superseded, now built (docs update 2026-11-25):** the settle-early slice is built and reviewed (`settle_difference` in `supabase/migrations/20261125090051_settle_difference.sql`, `settleDifference` in `lib/bills.ts`); a more-due row shows the payer a "Request $1.10" (SE-T6-4); ADR 0021 |
| VR-6 | After tapping Request | Nothing changes on screen: no "Requested" label, the row stays "Not yet"; a second tap within 1 s is ignored | A local "Requested" label until reload; a stored "requested at" | The app can't see whether the request was actually sent (the payer may cancel in Venmo), and a label that vanishes on reload would confuse. "Not yet" stays true until the person marks paid | Open. Built 2026-11-25 (guard: VR-P3) |
| VR-7 | Activity event | None; no DB change at all | Log "Hana requested $15.10 from Sam" | Nothing is verified, and an event would need a migration and a new kind for something that may not have happened | Open. Built 2026-11-25 (no DB change) |
| VR-8 | Owers with no Venmo username (or one that fails the pattern) | No button; muted caption "Hasn't added Venmo" | A disabled button with a hint | A disabled button reads as broken and is skipped or confusing for screen readers; a caption says why. The payer's own missing username doesn't hide the buttons (the request comes from whatever account is signed into Venmo); the existing "Add your Venmo" card stays | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| VR-9 | Web and desktop | The same button on every platform, plus one muted line under the list on web: "On a computer? Send requests from the Venmo app on your phone." | Hide Request on web; a QR code | Matches M3 Q8's answer for Pay. The `@handle` and the row's amount are what the payer needs to request by hand. What venmo.com does with `txn=charge` on desktop is unverified (User check U1) | Open. Built 2026-11-25; desktop and Android behavior still unverified (U1 not run) |
| VR-10 | Interaction with "I paid" | Requesting never marks anyone. When the person marks paid, their Request disappears live and Undo rules are unchanged; the payer still can't mark someone else paid (M3 Later list) | Let the payer mark "they paid my request" | Keeps ADR 0015's self-reported marks unchanged; "payer marks others paid" is a separate, already-listed feature | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| U-A | Request buttons | Per-row "Request" in the payer's Everyone list; individual requests only, no "Request all" (one Venmo link carries one amount). Wary of clutter | — | **Decided by the user (relayed), 2026-11-25.** Confirms VR-1 and VR-4 (spec Amendment A1) | Decided |
| U-B | Paying from the bill screen | For people who owe, the footer button opens the Venmo pay link directly (with the item-names note) instead of opening Settle up first | — | **Decided by the user (relayed), 2026-11-25.** Spec Amendment A1, design F | Decided |
| VR-11 | How the footer decides pay vs Settle | It shows the Settle card's own Pay action (same URL, label, notes) only when that card is in state `owes`; everything else keeps today's "Settle up" | A separate rule in the bill screen | One rule for both screens, so the fallbacks (nothing owed, negative, marked, no username, blocked, payer) can never disagree with the Settle page | Open. Built 2026-11-25 (`settleFooterAction`) |
| VR-12 | Footer label for owers | `Pay <Payer> $15.10 on Venmo` (the Settle page's existing string) | "Pay $15.10 on Venmo" | Says who gets the money before Venmo opens; one string for both screens | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| VR-13 | Handle line on the bill screen | `@hana-1 · $15.10` under the button, plus the existing "On a computer?" line on web | Leave it off for less clutter | Skipping Settle would otherwise remove the only place a wrong username is visible before money moves (M3-UX-14; before-production "mistyped username" item) | Open. Built 2026-11-25 (layout: VR-T5-1) |
| VR-14 | "I paid" after paying from the bill screen | After the Pay tap, the footer becomes one line: "Did you pay Hana?" with I paid / Not yet. Kept in screen state only, no app-state listening. "I already paid" (cash) stays on the Settle page | A banner at the top; no prompt (mark only on Settle) | Same pattern as the Settle page (M3-UX-1), in the spot the person just tapped; one line, no new screen | Open. Built 2026-11-25 (see also VR-T5-3). **Amended 2026-11-27 by Sent via Venmo (ADR 0024):** the footer's Pay records "Sent via Venmo" itself; "Did you send Hana $15.10?" (Yes, I sent it / Not yet) is only the fallback; "I already paid" became "Paid another way", and a Cash footer exists (SV-UX-10) |
| VR-15 | After marking from the bill screen | The footer goes back to "Settle up" with a muted "✓ Marked paid" caption | Hide the footer; keep a disabled Pay | Confirms the mark where it was made and still leads to the details | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| VR-16 | Reaching Settle up as an ower | A ghost "Details" link next to Pay (screen-reader label "Settle up details") | No link (only via Activity); a second full button | Owers still need the Settle page for "I already paid", undo and who's paid; a ghost link is the quietest control | Open. Built 2026-11-25 |
| VR-17 | Amount source on the bill screen | Server-confirmed data only (`settleViewInputFor(data, …)`), with Pay disabled while the viewer's claim, split or extras-mode write is in flight | Use the screen's optimistic shares | A Venmo amount must never come from a claim that hasn't committed (2026-11-22 lesson on optimistic updates) | Open. Built 2026-11-25; the "N left to claim" caption still follows the screen's optimistic counts, but Pay never does (`billFooterAction`, VR-I6 resolved below) |
| VR-18 | Web / popup blocking on the footer | Same as the Settle page: open first, no await before it; 1 s double-tap guard; the handle line is the fallback when a popup is blocked (undetectable) | A web-only "Open Settle up" instead | Same behavior on every platform (M3 Q8); the Safari popup before-production item is extended to this button | Open. Built 2026-11-25; before-production §5 Safari item extended (Safari check not run yet) |

### 2026-11-25 · Venmo Request buttons, Task 1 (implementer; `lib/paylinks.ts`)

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VR-I1 | Shape of the request builder | `venmoRequestUrlForFormat(format, input)` = `venmoLink(format, 'charge', input)`; `venmoRequestUrl(input)` uses `VENMO_LINK_FORMAT`. No other change to `venmoLink`; the pay functions and the note are untouched | A `txn` parameter on `venmoPayUrl` | Mirrors the pay pair exactly, as R1 asks; the one `venmo.com` template stays in `venmoLink` | Open |
| VR-I3 | Request row model shape (Task 2) | `request: { kind: 'link'; url; cents; handle; caption ('@handle'); label; a11yLabel; a11yHint } \| { kind: 'caption'; caption } \| null` on `SettleRowModel` | Spec's untagged union | A `kind` tag lets the screen switch without probing fields; the `@handle` caption is in the model so the component builds no strings | Open |
| VR-I4 | Q3 vs Q4 when both apply (bad username and amount over the cap) | Username first: an invalid or missing username shows "Hasn't added Venmo"; only a valid username with a null link shows "Request it in the Venmo app" | Amount first | The username is the thing the payer can fix (ask them to add Venmo); the amount caption only makes sense once a link could be built | Open |
| VR-I5 | Footer model shape (Task 3) | `settleFooterAction` returns null (F-a), `{ kind: 'settle', variant, caption, a11yHint }` (F-b/F-e/F-f, today's `settleFooter` fields), `{ kind: 'pay', button, notes, details }` (F-c, button and notes are the card's own objects) or `{ kind: 'did_you_pay', text, iPaid, notYet, details }` (F-d). Details and Not yet carry no `SettleAction`: the component knows they open Settle / clear `openedVenmo` | Add `details` / `not_yet` kinds to `SettleAction` | Adding kinds to `SettleAction` would force new cases into the Settle page's `onAction` switch (Task 4's file) for actions it never shows | Open |
| VR-I6 | F-b's "N left to claim" source | From the server-data plan (`plan.unclaimedRows`), per F1 | The bill screen's optimistic `shares.unclaimedRows` (today's footer) | F1 says the footer is built from server data. Task 5 should check whether the caption lagging an optimistic claim by one refetch is acceptable, or keep passing optimistic counts for F-b only | Replaced by Task 5's hybrid (row "VR-I6 (resolved by Task 5)" below) |
| VR-I7 | `settle.tsx` in Task 3 | `planFor` keeps its signature but its body is one call to `settleViewInputFor` (needed because `SettleViewInput` gained required `venmoOf` / `itemNamesOf`). `claimedItemNames`, `makeNameOf`, `splitInputFor`, `settlePlan` imports left the file | Leave `planFor` duplicated until Task 4 | One builder, as F1 asks; no behavior change (criterion 23 compares against the old body copied into the test) | Open |
| VR-I2 | Equivalence test inputs (criterion 2) | Fixed table: 2 formats × 9 usernames (5 and 30 chars, a normal one, 4 and 31 chars, `@hana-1`, `ab cd`, `null`, `''`) × 8 cents values (1, 1510, 99,999,999, 0, 100,000,000, −5, 12.5, NaN) × 6 name lists (absent, B1, B9, B15, B17, B18) = 864 checks. Random: 200 seeded inputs (seed 20261126), 80% valid usernames/cents, names from a pool that includes `?`, `=`, `&`, `%` and lone surrogates; the test also asserts more than 100 produced a URL, so it can't pass on `null === null` alone | Only the spec's literal list | The extra URL-special characters check that the note encoding can't produce a second `txn=` that the `replace` comparison would miss | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Venmo Request buttons, Task 5 (product-engineer; bill screen footer)

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VR-I6 (resolved by Task 5) | F-b's "N left to claim" source | Hybrid, in a new pure `billFooterAction(server, unclaimedRows, unclaimedDiscountRows)` in `lib/settleView.ts`: when the screen's optimistic counts show rows left, the footer is "N left to claim" from those counts at once (and never a Pay); when the screen shows nothing left but the server plan is still blocked (the last claim is saving), today's primary "Settle up" with no count; otherwise the server footer (Pay / Did you pay / Settle up). Pay amounts only ever come from server data | Server plan only (caption lags a claim tap by one refetch); optimistic only (Pay would use uncommitted claims) | No regression in responsiveness: the count moves with the tap exactly as before, while VR-17 still holds because the hybrid never builds a Pay from optimistic data | Open |
| VR-T5-1 | Footer layout for owers | Full-width primary Pay; under it one row with the muted `@handle · $X` (plus the web line on web) on the left and a ghost **Details** on the right. "Did you pay Hana Lee?" is one label line; under it **I paid** (primary), **Not yet** (ghost) and **Details** pushed right, so Details sits in the same spot in both states | Details on its own line; Details beside the question | Keeps the sticky footer one line taller than before (button + one caption row) and puts Details where the thumb already learned it is | Open |
| VR-T5-2 | What "disabled while saving" covers | Pay and I paid are disabled while the viewer has any optimistic claim, split or extras-mode entry on screen (from the tap until the refetch that includes it lands). Not yet and Details stay enabled (they write nothing) | Disable every footer control | The amount must come from committed data (VR-17); Not yet and Details can't send a wrong amount. If that refetch fails, the entry (and the disabled Pay) stays until the next successful refetch (live hint, focus, foreground): the same lifetime the optimistic claim already has on screen | Open |
| VR-T5-3 | "Did you pay?" after a mark | Cleared as soon as server data shows the viewer marked (so an Undo on Settle doesn't bring the prompt back on the bill screen); on iOS "Marked paid" is announced when the viewer's own mark lands; the "✓ Marked paid" caption is a polite live region for Android/web, and "N left to claim" is not (it would speak on every claim tap) | Keep the prompt state until unmount | The bill screen stays mounted under Settle, so a stale prompt could reappear after an Undo there | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Venmo Request buttons, review fixes (Amendment A2; main-session decisions, built by Task 5's product-engineer)

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VR-R1 | Bill screen: the refetch after the viewer's own saved claim/split/extras change fails | Pay and I paid stay disabled (the amount must come from data that includes the change), and the footer shows `Couldn't refresh. Try again.` (alert) with a ghost **Refresh** (hint "Loads the latest bill") that refetches. Shown only in the Pay / "Did you pay?" states, where it blocks something. Pure check `saveRefreshFailed(clearAts, version, finished)` in `lib/settleView.ts` | Re-enable Pay on a failed refetch; silent wait for the next live hint | Repeats lesson 2026-11-24 ("a failed refetch left the buttons stuck"): a failed second step needs a message and a retry, but a re-enabled Pay could send an amount from before the change | Open |
| VR-R2 | "Couldn't open Venmo…" lines after the bill changes | The screens keep what a failed open described (`{payerId, cents, handle}` for Pay, `{userId, cents, handle}` for Request) and show the line only while it still matches: `payOpenFailedLine(input, failure)` (same payer, the viewer still owes exactly that amount, same username) and `requestOpenFailedLine(people, failure)` (that row still offers the same request). Bill screen footer, Settle card and Settle list. The request link model gained `userId` for this | Clear the line on every refetch | A stale line would tell the person to send a wrong amount to a possibly wrong person; matching keeps the line through unrelated refetches (live hints) | Open |
| VR-R3 | Screen tests for live changes during "Did you pay?" | Added: share change → back to Pay with the new amount; payer change → Settle up; I paid disabled while a claim saves in that state (Not yet stays enabled) | — | Review nit | Open |
| VR-R4 | `settleRequestWebLine` | Takes `(input, people)`; the Settle screen passes the list it already built | Keep rebuilding `settlePeople` inside | One build per render | Open |
| VR-R5 | `planFor` in `settle.tsx` | Removed; the screen calls `settleViewInputFor` directly (also in the payer picker's fresh re-read) | Keep the pass-through | Nothing left in it | Open |
| VR-R6 | Request Q3 vs Q4 | The username rule lives only in `venmoLink`: a null request link with an amount outside C2 (`!isSettleCents`) reads Q4 "Request it in the Venmo app"; any other null link reads Q2/Q3 "Hasn't added Venmo". **Changes VR-I4** for the rare case of a bad or missing username *and* a share over $999,999.99: that now reads Q4 | Keep VR-I4 (username first) by testing the pattern in `requestFor` | One place for the username rule; the changed case needs a share over $1M | Open |

## Scan pipeline (web OCR preprocessing)
Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-22-web-opencv-preprocessing.md` (Amendments A1–A5, Results log, "Decision (proposed), 2026-11-24").

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-1 | What ships on web | Not decided yet. The user chose to check 3 more answer files (El Chalan, price_diff, quantity2case), fix `less-contrast`, then re-run Task 14. The likely result is crop + text-height resize | Ship crop only now (regresses El Chalan); crop + text-height now; nothing | **Waiting on user's answer checks** |
| S-2 | When the "Retake photo / Type it in" screen shows | Any failed quality check, including small mismatches | Only when no items or no total are found (small mismatches go straight to the form with the red banner) | Open |
| S-3 | Retry progress wording | "Taking a closer look…" (spec) | "Trying again…" | Open |
| S-4 | Web recognize time limit | 30 s (user) | 90 s; a stall-based limit | Kept |
| S-5 | Ranking of two reads (`pickBetter`) | ok > has items > enough lines > has total > smaller gap > fewer failed checks > more items > more lines | The old order (no-total could win) | Open |
| S-6 | Off by default (code kept, tested): grayscale/flatten/CLAHE, Tesseract thresholding/DPI/rotate/dictionaries, zoom on doubt, retry pairs, strips | Off, no measured gain on 3 tuning receipts | Ship any of them | Open (re-check with more receipts; strips need a real long receipt) |
| S-7 | OpenCV build | `@techstark/opencv-js@4.12.0-release.1` (10.9 MB), self-hosted, lazy, in a worker; custom minimal build before production | CDN; no OpenCV | Kept (user, A1) |
| S-8 | `(qty@unit)` notes accept 1 decimal (`2@3.5`), only inside the note | As picked (user) | 2 decimals only | Kept |

### 2026-11-25 · Scan pipeline docs (spec Task 15, docs-writer)
| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-D-1 | ADR numbers | **0018** (staged scan pipeline) and **0019** (OpenCV on web) | 0015 / 0016 as the spec and the main session assigned | 0015, 0016 and 0017 already existed (settling, people, claimable discounts). ADRs are never renumbered, so the next free numbers were used; both ADRs carry a numbering note | Open |
| S-D-2 | ADR status wording | "Accepted (architecture); what ships on web pending (S-1)" for both | "Proposed" until Task 14 is re-run | The pipeline and the OpenCV loading design are built and tested; only the shipping switch waits on the user | Open |
| S-D-3 | How the new docs link code | `path:line` **plus** the symbol name, with a note that lines are as of 2026-11-25 | Line numbers only (the agent file); symbol names only (pending proposal `2026-11-24-docs-cite-symbols-not-lines.md`) | The main session asked for `path:line`, and several sessions edit these files, so the symbol name keeps each link findable when lines move | Open |
| S-D-4 | `docs/before-production.md` | Added one sub-item under the recognize-timeout item: the 30 s OpenCV load limit and 60 s op limit, untested on phones | Leave it (the spec's six items were already covered by Task 14's three entries) | At slow mobile speeds an 11 MB download can exceed 30 s, so the crop would silently never run; that's a production-only risk nothing tracked | Open |
| S-D-5 | Skill proposal file name | `docs/lessons/proposals/2026-11-25-receipt-eval-skill-answers.md` (the main session's name) | `2026-11-22-receipt-eval-skill-update.md` (the spec's name) | Main session's instruction; the spec's file was never created | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Scan pipeline review fixes R1–R11 (implementer)
| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-R-1 | A `timeout` after the scan already has a read (text-height second pass, or the retry) | Keep the best read so far, add a `timeout` trace entry naming the step, show that read's screen (failure screen with pre-fill, or `/bill`). **No further pass or retry starts after an absorbed timeout.** A timeout before any read still shows "Reading took too long" | Start the retry anyway after a timeout in the primary's second pass | The web reader's worker is ended after a timeout, so a retry would restart it and likely time out again, doubling the wait for a worse-or-equal answer. Cancel still wins over a kept read | Open |
| S-R-2 | `CvOpFailed` in normalize / second pass / the encode before a read | Treated like `CvUnavailable`: fall back to today's path (prepare on the original photo; or the canvas image an enhanced handle came from), recorded as a `fallback`. A photo that won't decode (`decode_failed`) still fails with `image_load_failed`. The second pass now also records a normalize `fallback` when OpenCV fails there (before: silently "no_change") | Keep failing on op errors | The original photo is still good, so failing the scan threw away a read we could make. A decode failure means today's path can't read the photo either | Open |
| S-R-3 | Strip tiling whose crop can't leave the worker | Read the original photo whole (prepared by today's path), recorded as a recognize `fallback` | Fail the scan | Strips are planned in the crop's pixels, so they can't be cut from the photo; a whole read is today's behaviour | Open |
| S-R-4 | Answer-file totals check (`answers:check`) | The parser's rule and tolerance (±1¢ per check, via `totalsGaps` / `checkTotals`); `lib/receiptText.ts` not edited | Keep it exact (0¢) | CLAUDE.md: one money rule, one tolerance. A 1¢ typo in an answer file is no longer flagged; the eval scores items, not totals, so nothing else changes. `docs/setup/receipt-answers.md` updated | Open |
| S-R-5 | When "The first time takes a little longer" stops showing | Only after a scan whose trace has no `unavailable` and no `fallback` entry | Only cv-stage entries | Conservative: a fallback with another cause just shows the longer line once more; marking OpenCV loaded when it wasn't is the bug being fixed | Open |
| S-R-6 | Eval: a checked answer whose set isn't in `sets.json` (R7) | **Refuse to score it**: skipped with "set not recorded yet; run npm run answers:draft". An old `expected.json` counts as recorded (it carries its set) | Have the eval write the set into `sets.json` | Keeps the eval read-only and `answers:draft` the only writer of the set record (which only ever adds names). Recording from the eval could freeze a guess made from a stale state (e.g. before `--used-before-split` was given); refusing can't put a receipt in the wrong set. `answers:draft` records every photo's set even when its answer already exists, so the cost is one command | Open |
| S-R-7 | Eval: invalid held-out answer file (R6) | Prints only the file name and "details hidden until --confirm; npm run answers:check shows them" | Print nothing | The file name is needed to know something is wrong; the details quote the file's lines | Open |
| S-R-8 | Photo label with `--photos <dir>` (R11) | The real path (repo-relative when inside the repo), in both the eval's `photo` field and the draft header comment | Only the eval field | Same wrong label in both places; the header is what the user reads | Open |
| S-R-9 | `CV_WORKER_PATH` vs `OPENCV_SCRIPT` (R9) | Kept as they are, comments fixed: the worker path must be root-absolute (a relative `new Worker()` URL resolves against the SPA route), and `OPENCV_SCRIPT` is relative to the worker, so it follows it | Make both relative | A relative worker path breaks on nested routes such as `/bill/<id>/…` | Open |
| S-R-10 | Harness `Host` check (R8) | 403 for any `Host` other than exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` (so `localhost:<port>` is refused too) | Also allow `localhost` | Playwright opens `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`; nothing uses `localhost`, and an exact match is the simplest rule against DNS rebinding | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Scan pipeline QA gaps (criteria 41 and 45, implementer)
| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S-Q-1 | Where zoom on doubt's `hasPriceToken` lives (spec Task 13 asked for a read-only export from the parser) | A copy of the parser's money-token shape in `lib/scanPipeline/zoomOnDoubt.ts` (`PRICE_TOKEN_RE`, `countPriceTokens`, `hasPriceToken`, line 99 as of 2026-11-25), guarded by an agreement test in `zoomOnDoubt.test.ts` ("agrees with the parser": `Widget <token>` is an item exactly when it has a price token) | Export `hasPriceToken` from `lib/receiptText.ts` and import it | The parser was outside Task 13's files (and another session edits it), so the regex was repeated instead. Risk: two copies of one rule can drift; the agreement test catches drift only for the tokens it lists. Fix when the parser is next open: export the token from `lib/receiptText.ts` and delete the copy | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Venmo Request buttons, Task 4 (Settle screen; product-engineer)
Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-25-venmo-request-buttons.md` (A1, R2–R4, criteria 13–20).

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VR-P1 | Where Request sits on the row | A second line under the row: muted `@handle` on the left (under the name), small ghost **Request** flush right (under the "Not yet" column), the same spot and style as Undo | Request inline on the name/amount/status line | The first line already has three columns (name, amount, status); a fourth crowds it on a phone and wraps at large text sizes. Request and Undo never show on one row, so the right edge is always "the row's action" | Open |
| VR-P2 | Screen readers and the `@handle` text | The visible `@handle` is hidden from screen readers; the button's label already ends with it (`Request $15.10 from Sam on Venmo, @Tejesh-Bhaumik`) | Read both | Avoids hearing the handle twice in a row. The Q2/Q4 captions ("Hasn't added Venmo", "Request it in the Venmo app") are read, since nothing else says them | Open |
| VR-P3 | Double-tap guard scope | One 1 s guard across all Request buttons on the screen (its own timer, separate from Pay's) | One guard per row | A double tap that lands on a neighbouring row is still a double tap; tapping Sam then Dee a second apart works | Open |
| VR-P4 | The open-failed line | In the Everyone card's existing error banner (announced as an alert); cleared on the next Request tap or Undo | A per-row error | One error spot per card, as for Undo; the line names the handle and amount so it's clear which row it's about | Open |
| VR-P5 | Web line placement | One muted caption inside the Everyone card, under the last row, above any error | Under the card | Keeps it next to the buttons it explains, without adding a section | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Scan screen redesign (visual only; product-engineer)
Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-25-scan-screen-redesign.md`.

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SR-1 | Icon library | `lucide-react-native` 1.80.0 (pinned exactly) with `react-native-svg` (already installed). Icons imported one by one (`lucide-react-native/icons/camera`) so the bundle carries only the icons used. Jest maps those paths to lucide's CommonJS build (`package.json` `jest.moduleNameMapper`) | `@expo/vector-icons` (already installed; icon fonts, Ionicons/Material look); `expo-symbols` (real SF Symbols on iOS, but no Android/web icons, so a second fallback set would be needed; web fallback unverified) | One consistent, light line style on iOS, Android and web; small per-icon imports; no font loading. Cost: one new dependency | Open |
| SR-2 | Native manual-entry label | "Enter items" (was "Type it instead"), the same as web and the unavailable state | Keep "Type it instead" | The two choices are now equal cards; "instead" framed typing as the fallback. The M1a spec (criteria 40a/43, D9) still says "Type it instead"; the failure screen's "Type it in" is unchanged | Open |
| SR-3 | Choice layout | Two equal cards (icon in a soft circle, title, one-line hint), stacked on phones and side by side once each card gets 220 pt (wide phones in landscape, tablets, desktop web). No JS measuring: flex-wrap | Always side by side (hints wrap to 3–4 lines on a phone); two big buttons without hints | Stacked cards keep the hint on 1–2 lines and stay one-thumb friendly; side by side uses the room on wide screens | Open |
| SR-4 | Intro line under the title | "Start a bill from your receipt." | No intro; "How do you want to add the items?" | Says what the screen is for without repeating the cards' hints | Open |
| SR-5 | Card edge | Hairline `divider` border + the faint `shadow.card` token (new in `lib/theme.ts`) | The 3:1 `border` token | Calmer look; the card is identified by its icon and title, not its edge. If it reads too faint in bright light, switching to `border` is a one-line change | Open |

### 2026-11-25 · Visual pass V1–V4 (tokens, dark-mode backgrounds, sign-in/callback/invite, primitives; visual-designer)
Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-25-visual-pass.md` (§1, §3 V1–V4; P1 and P5 as approved by the user).

| # | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VP-1 | Google button look (P5) | Google's official light/dark themes: white pill with `#747775` 1-pt stroke and `#1F1F1F` text (light), `#131314` with `#8E918F` stroke and `#E3E3E3` text (dark), the four-color G at 20 pt, pill shape, full column width. Colors live in `lib/theme.ts` as `googleButtonColors` (brand-only, not in `Palette`). Same label, busy copy and behavior; while busy a spinner replaces the G | Google's "neutral" theme (`#F2F2F2`, no stroke); rectangular 4-pt corners | Light/dark follow the app's scheme; the pill is one of Google's two allowed shapes and the closer one to our rounded buttons | Open |
| VP-2 | Where the Google button departs from Google's spec | Height 44 (Google: 40) for our touch-target rule; the system font at 14/20 weight 500 instead of Roboto Medium (not bundled); the G drawn from Google's published path data with `react-native-svg` instead of the downloaded asset | Bundle Roboto with `expo-font`; ship the official PNG/SVG asset | No new font or asset dependency in a visual pass. Logged in `docs/before-production.md` §3 to check against the branding kit before launch | Open |
| VP-3 | "Back to sign-in" (callback) and "Go home" (invalid invite) | Kept as expo-router `Link`s, styled like a ghost button (label type, `primary`, 44-pt target from padding) | `Button variant="ghost"` with `router.navigate` (the spec's suggestion) | A `Button` would change the role from "link" to "button" and drop the web `<a href>`; styling the link keeps semantics and navigation identical. Two short style blocks; fold into `TextLink` (with an `href`) if a third appears | Open |
| VP-4 | Sign-in layout | Inside `Centered`: a 360-wide column (`layout.message`), "SplitIt" in `type.display`, the sentence in `body`/`textMuted` 4 pt below, then the Google button and any error 24 pt apart (section gap). The error is now `Banner tone="error" alert` (still announced as an alert; same copy) | Keep a bare red line | One focal point (the button); the error matches every other error in the app | Open |
| VP-5 | TopBar primitive | Not built: P1 (native stack headers) was approved, so pushed screens get the system header instead | Build it anyway for screens that can't use a native header | No screen needs it yet; build it only if one turns up | Open |
| VP-6 | SegmentedControl selected look | `primarySurface` fill, `primary` text at 700, on a `surfaceMuted` track with 2-pt inset and `radius.inner` segments; optional check icon (`checkSelected`) | The spec's "`surface` fill on a `surfaceMuted` track" | In dark mode `surface` (#161B22) is *darker* than `surfaceMuted` (#1F262F), so the selected segment would look like a hole, and in light mode white on #F1F3F5 is 1.1:1 and nearly invisible. Tint + weight + selected state is readable in both; the check is the non-color cue where a label could be misread | Superseded 2026-11-25 by VP-39: the selected segment is now a solid `primary` fill with white text (matches the tip chips). |
| VP-7 | ListRow grouping | Rows carry their own 16-pt side padding and an inset hairline; the containing `Card` is used with `padding: 0, gap: 0, overflow: 'hidden'` so the pressed background reaches the card's edges and follows its corners | A new `ListGroup` primitive; keep the card's padding (pressed background then stops short of the edge) | Avoids a second card-like primitive. V7 (Card adjust) can add a `flush` option so the five adopting screens don't repeat the override | Open |
| VP-8 | Focus ring | A theme token `focusRing` (2-pt solid outline, 2-pt offset, `colors.focus`) and a `useFocusRing` hook in `components/ui/`, used by every new primitive and the Google button. Rows draw it inset (`outlineOffset: -2`) | Leave the browser default | The browser default differs by element and `TextField` removes it; one ring everywhere. `ChoiceCard` still has its own copy (same values); switch it in V16 | Open |
| VP-9 | BillForm's local `IconButton`/`TextLink` | Left in place; the new `components/ui` versions are not adopted anywhere yet | Move BillForm to them now (spec V4) | The task said not to migrate screens yet; V10 switches BillForm (its delete glyph becomes an `X` icon then) | Open |
- VP-M1 (main session, 2026-11-25; picked without blocking per the user's build-without-blocking preference): back from the new-bill form (P2) — if nothing was entered, go straight back; if anything was entered, ask "Discard this bill?" (Discard / Keep editing) first. Alternatives: keep the draft for next time (more state to manage), discard silently (loses typing). Why: simplest, never loses work by surprise. Open for review.
