| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SV-R-1 | Copy when the write is refused after Venmo opened (R1) | `afterOpenRefusal`: "Venmo opened, but this was already marked. Don't send it. Check Venmo before you pay again." (created = false); "…who gets paid just changed. Don't send it. Check below before you pay." (payer_changed); "…your payment just changed. Don't send it. Check below before you pay." (mark_changed, and a "more" `not_allowed`); "…something on the bill just changed. Don't send it. Check your share below." (not_complete); "…this wasn't saved. Don't send it. Check Venmo before you pay again." (anything else). Always a refetch | Reuse the generic copy; one line for every case | Venmo is open and pre-filled: the line has to stop a second payment, and say why in the person's terms. D2 asks for copy that says to check Venmo | **Needs your look** (tone) |
| SV-R-2 | Closing the Venmo tab on web (R1) | On any refusal or `created = false` after the open, `w.close()` in a try (`closeOpenedTab`); the copy doesn't claim it closed | Leave it open | It's a tab we opened, so script may close it; if Venmo's app already took over, nothing happens and the line still says to check Venmo | Open |
| SV-R-3 | Network error, then Venmo fails to open (R4) | Re-read the bill (4 s limit); clear the viewer's mark only if it's `venmo`, to this payer, for this amount, and made at or after the tap less `LATE_SEND_SKEW_MS = 60 s` (starting value, `isLateOwnSend`). A failed re-read leaves it; the inline "Did it go through?" then covers it | Always unmark; never unmark | Never says "Sent" for a Venmo that didn't open, without touching an older genuine mark. The skew allows a server clock a little behind the phone | Open |
| SV-R-4 | Time limit on the unmark after a failed open (R10) | `UNDO_AFTER_FAILED_OPEN_TIMEOUT_MS = 4000` (starting value). Past it: the button comes back with "Couldn't undo. Try again."; a late unmark only refetches | No limit | A hung request kept the button busy for good | Open |
| SV-R-5 | Sign-out during a send (R10, R3c) | Session read (1 s limit) before scheduling the check-in, and again after it lands; changed → not scheduled, or cancelled. Venmo still opens when the mark was created | Don't open either | The mark exists and can't be undone without a session; opening keeps "Sent" true. Only the check-in, which would reach a signed-out device, is dropped | Open |
| SV-R-6 | How Settle up knows a "No, undo" was the viewer's own (R6, R8) | `lib/ownUndo.ts`: `runSentCheckUndo` notes the undo just before `unmarkPaid` (forgotten if it fails) and announces it once it lands. Settle up reads it when its mark vanishes (no "was undone… see who" banner) and refetches on the announcement. In memory only; an entry counts for `OWN_UNDO_MAX_MS = 2 min` | A route param; a one-shot flag in storage | Works whether the undo came from the card or the notification, with no URL-supplied state. The short life keeps a stale entry from hiding someone else's undo for long | Open |
| SV-R-7 | Settle up's card "No, undo" no longer sets `iUndid` (R6) | `iUndid` stays for the card's Undo only; the card's "No, undo" runs `runSentCheckUndo` and the registry keeps the banner away | Set `iUndid` around the runner | Before, iOS heard both "Undone. Not marked as sent." and "Marked not paid". Now just the first | Open |
| SV-R-8 | Responder when Settle up is already in front (R8) | No push; the page refetches through SV-R-6 | `router.replace` | No second copy of the page in the stack, and the page keeps its place | Open |
| SV-R-9 | Not received confirm (R2) | The confirm remembers the mark's `expectedAt` and `expectedCents`; a refetch with a different mark closes it (the link comes back with the new amount) | Keep it open with the new text | The payer confirmed one payment; it must not clear a different one | Open |
| SV-R-10 | The inline 30-minute window (R10) | `useSentCheckNow(markAt)` gives both screens a `now` that moves on a timer set for the window's end (`sentCheckInlineEndsInMs`), on focus, on return to the app, and when the mark changes | Re-render every minute | One timer per screen, only while a question could show | Open |
| SV-R-11 | Copy (R5) | Home Settled a11y "Settled up, everyone has settled up with you" (also in `__tests__/homeLayout.test.tsx` and two concept notes); a Venmo "more" landing announces "Sent $1.10 more"; cash / another way keep "Difference marked paid" | Keep "paid you" | "Paid" claims more than a Venmo open; "settled up" is the slice's word (SV-UX-14) | Open |
| SV-R-12 | Remembered methods (R9) | `isRememberedMethod` = a method with a chooser label (`PAYMENT_METHOD_COPY`) | Spell venmo/cash | Zelle or anything later joins by adding its chooser label | Open |

### 2026-11-27 · Sent via Venmo, QA fixes F1, F2, the web line, shared helpers, and useBill (product-engineer; `components/ui/SegmentedControl.tsx`, `components/ui/TextLink.tsx`, `components/ui/Button.tsx`, `lib/ariaProps.ts` (new), `components/SettlePeopleList.tsx`, `lib/settleView.ts`, both bill screens, `lib/asyncTimeout.ts` (new), `lib/webEnv.ts` (new), `lib/useVenmoSend.ts`, `lib/useBill.ts`)

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SV-WEB-1 | Where the "On a computer?" lines show | Desktop web only (`platform === 'web'` and not `isMobileWeb()`), for all three: under Pay ("Send it from the Venmo app on your phone"), under Request on a back-due card and the payer's list ("Send requests…"), and the list's "Open Venmo on your phone to pay or request". The screens pass `mobileWeb` as a device fact; it defaults to false | Hide only the Pay line (the one QA named); reword the line for phones | On a phone's browser the person is already on their phone, so every "On a computer?" line reads oddly, not just the Pay one. Group Settle already shows its lines on desktop only (`GroupSendPlatform`). **Needs your look: QA named one line; this hides all three** | Open |
| SV-WEB-2 | Web aria for a segment in button mode (F1) | `aria-pressed` on web (a toggle button), `accessibilityState.selected` on native | `aria-selected`, as the QA note suggested | ARIA allows `aria-selected` only on option, tab, row and gridcell, so browsers drop it on `role="button"` and axe reports it. `aria-pressed` is what a screen reader reads as "selected" for a toggle. Radios use `aria-checked` (typed, and the same `checked` on native) | Open |
| SV-WEB-3 | How primitives reach the DOM's aria state (F1) | `lib/ariaProps.ts` mirrors an `accessibilityState` into `aria-*` props (only the states that are set); TextLink spreads it, Button passes `aria-busy` / `aria-disabled` only when true, SegmentedControl passes `aria-checked` / `aria-pressed` / `aria-disabled`. React Native merges the same props into `accessibilityState`, so native is unchanged | Fix each call site; a web-only wrapper component | One rule for every primitive; the 20-odd hand-built controls that still set only `accessibilityState` (e.g. `ClaimItemRow`, `AssignChips`, `TipOptions`) are outside this task and keep the gap on web | Open |
| SV-WEB-4 | Confirm buttons at 200% zoom / large text (F2) | Both inline confirms on the Everyone list (Not received, Undo): the button row wraps, each button has `flexShrink: 1, maxWidth: '100%'`, the label wraps inside (Button already had `flexShrink` on its label). Height stays 44 | Smaller padding; stack the buttons always | Keeps the normal-size look; only a button wider than the Banner's text column changes | Open |
| SV-WEB-5 | Shared helpers for the Groups session | `within` + `TIMED_OUT` in `lib/asyncTimeout.ts`; `isMobileWeb` in `lib/webEnv.ts` (new module, not `lib/paylinks.ts`). `lib/useVenmoSend.ts` re-exports all three | Put `isMobileWeb` in `lib/paylinks.ts` | `paylinks.ts` builds and opens payment links; which browser this is isn't about links, and screen tests mock `paylinks` (partly), which a detection helper there would get caught in | Open |
| SV-WEB-6 | `useBill`'s `finished` (coordinator; the Groups review found it in a twin hook) | A refetch that a newer one has superseded no longer counts as finished; only the newest refetch sets `finished`. Waiters on it wait for the newer one | Keep `finished` as is and have every waiter also check "superseded" | The newer refetch started after the write, so it covers it; one rule in the hook instead of in every waiter. Before, Sam back from Venmo could see "Couldn't refresh" and miss the "Marked paid" announcement while the newer refetch was still on its way. If the newest refetch never answers, waiters still wait (the open "never arrives" question, 2026-11-24) | Open |

### 2026-11-27 · Sent via Venmo, QA re-check N1–N3 and the busy fix (product-engineer; `components/ui/SegmentedControl.tsx`, `components/SettleCard.tsx`, `components/SettleFooter.tsx`, `lib/useVenmoSend.ts`, `lib/settleView.ts`, both bill screens)

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SV-RC-1 | Chooser at 200% zoom (N1) | The track gets `flexWrap: 'wrap'`, `flexShrink: 1`, `maxWidth: '100%'`. Each segment gets `flexShrink: 1` and `flexGrow: 1` (still min 44 pt wide, 40 + slop tall). When the labels don't fit, Cash wraps under Venmo and each segment fills its row. Checked in Chromium at 160 and 187 px (inside the card) and at 375 px (one row that hugs its labels, as before) | Shrink only (a word like "Venmo" can't wrap, so it would overflow); stack the segments vertically always; smaller padding | Keeps the normal-size look (SV-VIS-1). Without the grow, the wrapped segments had different widths and an empty strip of track beside them. A hugging track has no spare width, so the grow does nothing at normal sizes. It applies to every hugging SegmentedControl (TipOptions % / $, GroupPayCard, GroupPaymentForm) | Open |
| SV-RC-2 | Radio keyboard on web (N2) | Radio mode on web only: roving tabindex (the checked radio is 0, the rest -1; the first is 0 if none is checked). Space selects the focused radio. Arrow Left/Up and Right/Down move the selection and focus, wrapping round. `preventDefault` on handled keys so the page doesn't scroll. Disabled: no keys act. Button mode and native are unchanged. Tested in jsdom with react-native-web (`__tests__/segmentedRadioKeyboardWeb.test.tsx`) and in Chromium on the built app | Arrows move focus only (manual activation); handle the keys on the radiogroup | WAI-ARIA's radio-group pattern selects as focus moves. Handling the keys per radio gives the index directly, and react-native-web's Pressable passes `onKeyDown` through | Open |
| SV-RC-3 | Saved, but the screen couldn't refresh (N3) | In `useVenmoSend`: when a write landed (a share, "more", cash, another way, a difference, or "Yes, I sent it") and the refetch after it failed, `held` is set. Every write through the hook is refused until a refetch lands. That can be Try again, a realtime hint, focus or a return to the app. Settle up's card shows a warning Banner (role alert) with Try again, and disables all its controls without a spinner. The bill footer swaps its refresh line for the same words with Try again, and disables Pay / cash / I paid / Yes / No, undo. When the hold clears, iOS announces what landed | Optimistic "Sent"; re-enable the button with only a message | No optimistic state (lessons 2026-11-22, 2026-11-24). Re-enabling the button invited a second payment. This replaces the R1 rule "a failed refetch re-enables the button" for writes that landed; refused writes still re-enable it. Three tests that pinned the old rule were updated (`billScreen` 31, `settle` R1 + N3 and "the write works but the refetch fails") | Open |
| SV-RC-4 | What Try again does while held (N3) | It starts a refetch and keeps Pay held while that runs (the button spins). A failed retry leaves the message and the hold in place. Only a refetch that lands releases Pay | Release Pay as soon as Try again is tapped, as the QA note allowed | Releasing on the tap would return the screen to "You owe / Pay" with no message if the retry also failed: the same trap. Way out if the network stays down: Details or Back, then come back (a fresh load). **Needs your look: this is stricter than the note's "or until the person taps Try again"** | Open |
| SV-RC-5 | Held copy (N3) | "Your payment was saved, but we couldn't refresh. Try again before you pay again." (`SETTLE_COPY.savedNotRefreshed`). Button "Try again", hint "Loads the latest bill". Warning tone (the payment is saved; nothing failed that the person must redo) | "…Pull to refresh before paying again." (QA's example) | There is no pull-to-refresh on these screens, and the button beside the text is labelled Try again. "Payment" covers Venmo, cash and another way. **Needs your look: copy** | Open |
| SV-RC-6 | Only the pressed button is busy (minor) | `SendBusy` carries the pressed action's `action` kind and `method`; Settle up passes `[busy.action]` and `busyMethod` to the card, which spins only the button with that kind and method. The others are disabled, not busy | Separate busy flags per button | Cash and "Paid another way" are both `mark`, and Pay more and "I paid the difference" were both `difference`, so one press used to set `aria-busy` on two buttons | Open |

## 2026-11-26 · Home groups v2 (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-26-home-groups-v2.md](specs/2026-11-26-home-groups-v2.md))
PM decisions on the user's redefinition of Home (Waiting on you, Owed to you, Settled, hidden; "Recent" removed). User rules that bind these are in the spec ("The user's rules"). Rule 7 (group by what's owed, markers decide only the pill) is the user's own reversal of an earlier marker-first idea.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GV-1 | What decides the group | Only the viewer's balance on the bill (owes > 0, owed > 0, both 0) and role, read off the settle plan (`viewerBalance`). Markers decide only the pill | Group on marker kinds (the old `WAITING_MARKERS`); marker-first for Settled | User rules 6 and 7. One input for both pill and group (the plan), and a consistency record (`MARKER_GROUPS`) proves they agree | Open |
| GV-2 | Payer who is owed and also owes a pay-back | Owed to you (owed is checked before owes) | Waiting on you (you have to pay someone) | Matches the pill's own priority (collecting first) and the user's example (a new person claims on a settled bill → "back to Owed to you"). The Settle screen shows both | Open |
| GV-3 | Guest whose payer owes them money back (`paid_back_due`) | Owed to you | Waiting on you; hidden | Money is owed to the viewer, so it isn't zero (rule 6); "Owed to you" says exactly that | Open |
| GV-4 | Negative shares settled outside the app (unmarked `payer_owes_them`) | Don't count toward the balance | Count them (bill never settles or hides) | The app can never record them as settled; a marked person's pay-back still counts | Open. **Money: worth a look** |
| GV-5 | Before the plan lands, or after a failed settle read | Guest → Waiting on you (no pill); payer → Owed to you. Hidden and Settled only once a plan confirms zero | Hide or settle optimistically; hold the list | Never hide or collapse on a guess; the list never waits (SE-UX-14). Cost: rows move on first load and after a failed read (Risks 1, 2) | Open. **Worth a look**. *Amended by GV-24 (user, A1.M3 and S5): the first load now waits up to 1000 ms, and a failed refresh keeps the previous plans. The fallback applies only after a timeout or a failed first read* |
| GV-6 | Payer bills with no `ready` plan (incomplete, blocked, all-$0, bad payer, early) | Owed to you, even a "Just you" bill | Waiting on you; Settled | Not complete means money is still to be collected; Settled needs a `ready` plan | Open |
| GV-7 | Payer, ready plan, nobody owes anything, no marker (e.g. a solo bill fully claimed) | Settled | Owed to you | "Everyone has paid" holds vacuously; Owed to you with nothing owed would be false | Open |
| GV-8 | Guest who owes $0 (including a guest who claimed nothing on a complete bill) | Hidden | Waiting on you | Zero in both directions (rule 4) | Open. *Changed by review R2 (2026-11-27), see the build notes below:* on a plan still being claimed (early), a viewer who has claimed nothing goes to Waiting on you, not hidden |
| GV-9 | Early (still being claimed) plans | Balances count as they are; a marked guest whose early share moved comes back | Defer early differences until complete | Rule 6. Unclaimed items keep their weight, so others claiming doesn't move a share; only real changes do. The pill still reads "Paid $X" (dependency D-2) | Open |
| GV-10 | A payer who isn't the host and hasn't opened the bill (`new`) | Owed to you, with the "New" pill | Waiting on you | Grouping is by balance (rule 7); the accent pill still asks for a look | Open |
| GV-11 | Settled open/closed state | Collapsed on every Home mount; kept while Home is mounted (visiting a bill, focus refresh); not stored on the device; collapses again when the count drops to 0 | Remember on the device | Calm default, nothing to store or migrate; the count tells you what's inside | Open |
| GV-12 | The Settled toggle's role and copy | Visible `Settled (5)` styled like a section heading; role button with `expanded`; label `Settled, 5 bills`; hints `Shows bills everyone has paid you for` / `Hides settled bills`; ≥ 44 pt tall; no fill | Header role; a ghost button "Show settled" | React Native gives one role; the expanded state needs button. No teal: it isn't the next step | Open. *Look changed by GV-21 (A1.M1):* a teal "Show"/"Hide" word and chevron on the right; role, label, hints and height as here |
| GV-13 | Home when nothing is waiting or owed | A card "You're all caught up." plus "Bills you've paid don't show here." when any are hidden; Settled toggle below if any | Only the Settled row; keep "Your bills" heading | Says why the list looks short; no extra heading | Open |
| GV-14 | Group order and order inside groups | All-caught-up card (if shown), Waiting on you, Owed to you, [Groups slot for splitit-app-52], Settled; list order inside each | Owed first; news first | Your move first; done last; no reshuffling (HR-9) | Open |
| GV-15 | "Claimed · waiting on others" | A new muted marker `waiting_on_others` (dependency D-1, built by the homeMarkers owner): guest, claimed, plan `blocked` / `blocked_all_zero`; a11y "You've claimed. You can pay once the bill is ready" | A Home-only pill in `lib/homeSections.ts`; no pill | Pills live in `lib/homeMarkers.ts`; same group either way, so it can land later | Open. *Pill text decided by the user (A1.S7):* `Claimed · not ready to pay`. Built by the Sent-via-Venmo session in `lib/homeMarkers.ts` |
| GV-16 | Viewer missing from a ready plan | Waiting on you (guest) / Owed to you (payer) | Hidden | Never hide on missing data | Open |
| GV-17 | `WAITING_MARKERS` | Removed; replaced by `MARKER_GROUPS: Record<HomeMarker, HomeGroup[]>` (a consistency check, not the rule) | Keep both | One record; adding a marker kind fails typecheck until it has an entry (the old hand-written list didn't) | Open |
| GV-18 | "Recent" | Removed, with its constant | Keep for payer bills | User rule 5 | Kept (user) |
| GV-19 | A guest who sent their whole share via Venmo (92) | Hidden, like any paid-up guest bill | Keep in a "Recent"-like group with "Sent $15.10" | User rule 4; 92's UX mock-ups updated through the main session | Open |
| GV-20 | Hidden bills and the 20-bill list | Accepted for now (Q2 to the user) | Change `my_bills()` | Needs a migration in another session's files | Open; Q2 |

**Amendment A1 (UX review, 2026-11-26; spec §A1).** The user decided M2 ("To collect $X"), M3 (first load waits up to 1000 ms), S5 (keep plans on a failed refresh) and S7 (pill "Claimed · not ready to pay"). Those are recorded as user decisions below. Skipped for later: C1, C2, C4, C5, C6.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GV-21 | Shape of the Settled toggle | Shared `components/ui/DisclosureRow.tsx` used directly by Home (no `SettledToggle` wrapper); teal "Show"/"Hide" + ChevronDown/ChevronUp; never ChevronRight | A Home-only toggle; a thin wrapper | One disclosure language for Settled and 52's "Hidden groups"; a right chevron means "opens a screen" on Home | Open |
| GV-22 | "To collect" when the payer also owes pay-backs | Gross amount still coming in (unpaid shares + `more` differences); `back` differences not subtracted | Net (in minus back) | The figure answers "how much is coming to me"; a net could be $0 while people still owe | Open. **Money: worth a look** |
| GV-23 | Where "To collect" shows | Payer, `ready` plan, group Owed to you only; early/blocked/no-plan payer rows and Settled rows keep "Total" | Also early plans | Early shares can still move (HR-3's reasoning) | Open |
| GV-24 | GV-5 after A1 | First load: wait ≤ 1000 ms, then GV-5's fallback; first-read failure: fallback at once; refresh failure: keep previous plans | GV-5 as first written | User decisions M3 and S5 | Kept (user) |
| GV-25 | Pill on rows inside Settled | Hidden visually; the row's screen-reader label keeps "Settled up, everyone has paid you" | Show it | The toggle already says it; the label keeps context because the toggle isn't a heading | Open; label wording changed by SV-R-11 (2026-11-27): "Settled up, everyone has settled up with you" |
| GV-26 | Scroll on opening Settled | Smallest scroll that shows the first settled row, only on open, `animated: !reduceMotion`; never on close | No scroll; scroll to the end | On a small phone the opened card can sit under the sticky bar | Open |
| GV-27 | Motion on Home | None for group changes or the Settled open/close; only S2's scroll animates, off under Reduce Motion | Fade or layout animation | Happens on every open; motion would spotlight a correction (review §5) | Open |
| GV-28 | First-load wait value | `FIRST_SETTLE_WAIT_MS = 1000` (starting value), first load of each Home mount only | 500 ms; no cap | User's number; tunable with evidence from U1 | Kept (user) |

**Build notes (2026-11-27, docs-writer, after review round 1 and QA for Tasks 1–2).** These rows now match the built code:
- **User decisions (2026-11-26, in chat):** the user decided M2 ("To collect $X", GV-22, GV-23), M3 (the first load waits up to 1000 ms, GV-24, GV-28), S5 (a failed refresh keeps the previous plans, GV-24) and S7 (the pill `Claimed · not ready to pay`, GV-15). The rest of A1 was accepted by the main session.
- **Review R2 changed one D3 cell (and GV-8).** The spec put a guest with a zero balance on an early plan (`nothing`, still being claimed) in hidden. The build sends it to **Waiting on you** when the viewer has claimed nothing and the plan isn't final (`!b.final && !summary.claimedAny` in `homeGroup`). That's user rule 1 ("nothing claimed yet" is Waiting on you). In practice it's a host who isn't the payer and hasn't claimed. A guest bill is still hidden on a final (`ready`) plan with a zero balance, or once the viewer has claimed and owes nothing. `homeGroup`'s input now also takes `claimedAny` from the summary.
- **Review R6 changed criterion 29a's scroll formula (GV-26).** `toggleY` is the top of the Settled block, which is the toggle's label line, because of its negative margins. So the target is `toggleY + TOUCH_TARGET + 2 × DISCLOSURE_ROW_MARGIN + space.sm + ROW_MIN_HEIGHT + space.lg − visibleHeight` (`settledScrollTarget` in `app/(app)/index.tsx`). The spec's version left out the two margins (−26 pt) and overshot. Review R4 also moved the scroll to the opened card's `onLayout`.
- **Rename (GV-22):** the spec's `collectCents(plan, myId)` was built as `stillToCollectCents`. `lib/settle.ts`'s ready plan already has a `collectCents` field (the sum of every positive share, paid or not), and one name for two different sums would confuse readers.
- **Review R3 (GV-24):** a kept plan is dropped on refresh when the new list names a different payer for that bill. Otherwise the old plan would win in `viewerIsPayer`.
- **Review R1 (GV-12, GV-21):** `DisclosureRow` passes `aria-expanded` as well as `accessibilityState`, because react-native-web 0.21 drops the latter. `components/SharesSummary.tsx` has the same gap (`docs/before-production.md` §7).

## 2026-11-26 · Who paid (spec: [docs/specs/2026-11-26-who-paid.md](specs/2026-11-26-who-paid.md))
PM decisions under "keep building". Full reasoning is in the spec's "Decisions (WP-)" table. Rows marked **needs your look** are listed at the end of the spec.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP-1 | Who can be a payer | Only people on the bill; alone after a scan, the sheet offers "Add people" | Anyone you've split with, added to the bill automatically | Keeps invariant P; no half-failing two-step write | Open |
| WP-2 | Data model | Keep `bills.payer_id` (one person / lead) + `bill_payers` rows only when split | Always rows; drop `payer_id` | No migration of existing bills; one-person bills never go stale | Open |
| WP-3 | Where marks live | `bill_marks` per (from, to) pair; 92's columns move there | Direction + counterparty columns on `bill_participants` | One person can owe two people | Open |
| WP-4 | Who pays whom | Each non-payer pays one payer (capacity greedy); payers settle among themselves; marks sticky | Plain fewest-payments greedy; everyone through the lead | One Venmo link per non-payer; paid people never re-routed; single payer = today | Open, **needs your look** |
| WP-5 | Single-payer regression | Identical objects, proven against a fixture captured before the change | Accept small differences | User asked for exactly today's results | Open |
| WP-6 | $0 / negative-share non-payers | Assigned to the lead; negative settles outside the app as today | Greedy routing | Same as today | Open |
| WP-7 | Marks when payers change | Clear marks to anyone no longer a payer (confirm + token); amount-only changes keep marks | Clear all; lock once marked | Today's rule generalised | Open, **needs your look (money)** |
| WP-8 | Settle early on split bills | Not allowed | Early, unclaimed sits with the lead | Early shares don't sum to the total | Open |
| WP-9 | Total edited after a split | Settling blocked until the host fixes amounts | Auto-adjust the lead's amount | Never guess who paid the extra | Open, **needs your look (money)** |
| WP-10 | One-person amount | Not stored (= current total) | Store it | Can't go stale | Open |
| WP-11 | Tolerance | Exact, 0¢ | ±1¢ | Typed amounts have no rounding (as GR-5) | Open |
| WP-12 | Blank or 0 in the sheet | "Didn't pay"; invalid never counts | 0 is an error | Most people paid nothing | Open |
| WP-13 | Saving | One person: a tap saves (confirm when needed); split: explicit Save | Save in both | Matches today's picker | Open |
| WP-14 | Split prefill | Stored amounts; blank when switching from one person, plus "Add the rest" | Prefill the payer with the total | Avoids starting "over the total" | Open |
| WP-15 | Co-payer leaving / removed | Refused until who paid changes (L3) | Allow and revert | Leaving never changes money | Open |
| WP-16 | Co-payer account deleted | Back to one person (host), K clear, actor-null event | Block deletion | Mirrors M3-9 | Open (before-production) |
| WP-17 | Line for 4+ payers | "Paid by 4 people" | List all | Short line | Open |
| WP-18 | Settle's PayerPicker | Replaced by the same line + sheet | Keep both | One place to edit | Open |
| WP-19 | Footer when you owe 2+ people | "Settle up" with caption, no Pay | Pay the larger | Two links can't be one primary | Open |
| WP-20 | Event | New `payers_changed`; one → one keeps `payer_changed` | Widen `payer_changed` | Old details parse unchanged | Open |
| WP-21 | Lead | Largest amount, ties to lower id; computed in the DB only | Host; first entered | Deterministic, one implementation | Open |
| WP-22 | Who edits | Host only (M3-1) | Any payer | Unchanged | Open |
| WP-23 | When editable | Any time, with confirms | Lock once complete | User wants it editable | Open |
| WP-24 | Minimum total to split | 2¢ | 1¢ | Two people at ≥1¢ | Open |
| WP-25 | `my_bills()` | Not touched by this slice; ask 92 to leave `not_received_*` out (Home reads flags from settle data). Fallback: re-create after 92 and Groups T5, keeping both | Re-create it in 4a regardless | Three sessions editing one function is the riskiest overlap | Resolved (eng review S15): 92's `my_bills()` adds only `seen_at`; never touched |

**Amendment A1 (2026-11-26): user decisions and reviews.** See spec §A1.
- **Changed by the user:**
  - WP-4 → fewest payments overall; one person may pay two; routes change only when the bill changes.
  - WP-7 → payments ledger: recorded payments are never cleared; holders pass money on.
  - WP-9 → kept (amounts must add up; settling pauses), with past payments counting.
- **Changed by A1:**
  - WP-3 → `bill_payments` entries + `bill_payment_flags`.
  - WP-16 → BEFORE DELETE trigger; other people's payments kept; `lost` count.
  - WP-19 → WP-48.
  - WP-20 → WP-37.
- WP-1, 2, 5, 6, 8, 10–15, 17, 18, 21–24 stand.

**Amendment A2 (2026-11-27, docs Task 12 note): the page replaced the sheet.** The user chose a separate "Who paid?" page over the bottom sheet (WP-53, spec A2.1). Checked in the code on 2026-11-27: `app/(app)/bills/[id]/payers.tsx` exists; `components/PayersSheet.tsx` and `components/ui/Sheet.tsx` don't; `components/PayerPicker.tsx` is deleted. So these rows describe a design that wasn't built, and the page's own rows (WP-PAGE-1 … 8, WP-R9, WP-R10) apply instead:
  - WP-1's "the sheet offers Add people": the page shows `Others show up here once they join.` and no Add people link.
  - WP-13, WP-14, WP-44 and WP-50 (sheet saving, prefill, segment names, "Sam paid it all"): replaced by the page's $ / % rows and one Save (WP-PAGE-4, WP-PAGE-5).
  - WP-17 (4+ payers line): A1.5 changed it to `Paid by Hana, Sam and 2 others` for non-hosts.
  - WP-18: Settle's PayerPicker was replaced by the `PayerLine` ("Paid by … · Change") and the page, not by a sheet.
  - WP-49 (`Sheet.tsx`): not built.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP-26 | Ledger storage | `bill_payments`: one immutable row per recorded movement, plus `bill_payment_flags` | A running net per pair | Route stability needs to know which payments were made under the current bill state; a running net loses that | Open |
| WP-27 | How routes stay stable | Each payment stores the client's plan key (a fingerprint of shares and paid amounts); payments under the current key only reduce their route; anything else is folded into positions and re-planned | Server bill revision (triggers on claims, items, bills, payers: lock contention); the Activity log (some edits write no event); a persisted plan (the DB can't compute shares) | No new locks; an item rename doesn't re-route; a wrong key can only re-plan, never change money | Open |
| WP-28 | Fewest-transfers algorithm | Greedy: smallest position pays largest, ties to the lower id (same as Groups GR-3), after pins and refunds; ≤ n−1 | An exact search | Deterministic, shared with Groups; optimal is NP-hard | Open |
| WP-29 | A non-payer who overpaid | Refunded by the person they paid | Routed to whichever debtor greedy picks | Matches today's pay-back; keeps single-payer results identical | Open |
| WP-30 | Negative-share non-payers | Pinned to the lead, settled outside the app (WP-6 kept) | Greedy routing | Same as today | Open |
| WP-31 | Payer changes | Clear no payment; `set_payer` loses its expected-count token; flags to removed payers deleted (92's rule) | Keep the token | Nothing is lost, so a stale confirm only explains less | Open |
| WP-32 | Payer-change confirm | Only when payments move; explains who passes what on; primary button, not danger | No confirm | Money routes change for named people | Open |
| WP-33 | Undo and Not received | Delete the current mark's run of entries; a pay-back is a recorded movement and is kept | Insert reversing entries | Undo and Not received say the money never moved | Open |
| WP-34 | Mark time | The start of the pair's latest positive run | The first ever entry | A re-mark after a full pay-back is a new send (92's check-in key) | Open |
| WP-35 | Who may receive | `mark_paid` and "more" only to current payers; a pay-back by the pair's recipient, who may be an ex-payer | Any participant | Keeps today's `payer_changed` guard; lets ex-payers return money | Open |
| WP-36 | Leaving | Allowed only when every pair involving you nets to $0 (plus the existing rules) | Any entry blocks | Someone fully paid back may leave, as today | Open |
| WP-37 | Event | Every payer change logs `payers_changed {before, after, moved, lost?}`; old `payer_changed` still parsed | Keep `payer_changed` for one → one | `moved` is needed for every change | Open |
| WP-38 | Telling affected people (U4) | Activity lists the moved payments; the sender's and holder's cards and footer say so, derived from the plan | A stored notice | Nothing to store or dismiss; always matches the plan | Open |
| WP-39 | Early single-payer plans | Unclaimed counts as the lead's share for routing only | Exclude early | Positions must sum to 0; one payer = today | Open |
| WP-40 | Plan key format | 16 lowercase hex characters, or null (= made under an earlier state) | A longer hash | Short, checkable; collisions only re-plan | Open |
| WP-41 | Testing the data move | A scratch Supabase stack in a scratchpad copy; ask the user before any `db reset` only if that fails | `db reset` on the shared local DB | Doesn't wipe the user's local data | Open |
| WP-42 | Line action word (U1) | `Change` | `Edit` | `Edit items` sits right below; Settle and 92 say Change | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-43 | `you` case (U2) | `Paid by you` (lowercase mid-sentence) | `Paid by You` | A capital reads like a name | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-44 | Segments (U3) | `One person` / `Several people` | `Split the paying` | "Split" already means item and extras splits | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-45 | Notify affected people (U4) | Yes, Activity + their bill (WP-38) | Activity only | Avoids paying twice | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-46 | Amounts in the line (U5) | Kept | Names only | An honest ledger at a glance | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-47 | Two "You owe" cards (U6) | One filled button: the first unmarked card | Both filled | One primary per screen | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-48 | Footer when you owe 2+ (U7) | Primary `Settle up` + `You owe 2 people · $30.00` | Secondary (old WP-19) | Secondary means "done" in 92's footer | Open (reviewer's recommendation) |
| WP-49 | Sheet shell | Extract `components/ui/Sheet.tsx`, move ItemSheet onto it (coordinate with Groups T17) | Copy the shell | Two sheets; keyboard and dirty guard in one place | Open |
| WP-50 | One person entered the whole total in Several people | `That's one person paying the whole bill.` + `Sam paid it all` (saves as one person) | An error | Turns a dead end into the right save | Open |

## 2026-11-26 · Groups (plan: docs/plans/groups-2026-11-26.md)
- Decided by the user: group-level settling only; everyone signs in; a group receipt counts only once fully claimed (unclaimed items never count against the payer); simplified payments by default; privacy — group membership counts for People you've split with, members see Venmo usernames, group name in Venmo notes behind an off switch.
- GR-M1 (main session): MVP splits = Equal + Exact (Shares later). Alternative: include Shares now. Why: smallest MVP; the PM and Engineer disagreed. Open.
- GR-M2 (main session): an expense's creator or its payer may edit/delete it. Alternative: any member. Why: fewer surprise edits on money. Open.
- GR-M3 (main session): no push reminders in the MVP — they need a server/scheduled job (ADR 0010); backend decision left to the user. Open.
- Decided by the user (2026-11-26, relayed by the coordinator): on a bill in a group, any group member can assign an item (or a share of it) to another member; non-group bills keep "users write only their own claims". People join a group by link or QR and then see all of its bills.

PM decisions for the spec ([docs/specs/2026-11-26-groups-mvp.md](specs/2026-11-26-groups-mvp.md)), made under "keep building". Contracts C-G1…C-G12 and tasks T0–T19 are in the spec.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-1 | Which bills count (C-G1) | Only fully claimed and not all-$0 (user's U-G3), lib only; the DB computes no balances | Plan's rule G (proportional counts early, unclaimed sits with the payer) | User decision. Kept one-sided on purpose so nobody "fixes" the gap (K6 lesson) | Kept (user) |
| GR-2 | Where balances and suggestions are computed | `lib/` only, from one snapshot read (plan option A) | Split math in SQL (option B); client-written share snapshots (C) | No second copy of `computeShares`; payments are honor-system anyway | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-3 | Tiny suggestions | Greedy (largest creditor ↔ largest debtor, ties to lower user id); suggestions ≤ `GROUP_IGNORE_CENTS` (starting value 1¢) hidden; "You're settled up" when you have none | Show every cent (0); drop ±1¢ nets before simplifying (can leave a debtor with nobody to pay) | Matches SE-11's ±1¢; the full list still settles to $0 and the leftover is bounded (C-G3) | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-4 | Equal split leftover cents | Computed by the DB with `private.allocate`, users in user-id order, leftover to the lowest ids; lib previews with `allocate` | Leftover to the payer; computed by the client | Same rule both sides, same as `lib/split.ts` ties | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-5 | Exact amounts | Must add up to the cent, no tolerance; $0 people left out of the payload | ±1¢ like the bill form | Typed amounts have no rounding, so any gap is a typo | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-6 | Putting a new receipt in a group | `create_bill` unchanged, then `attach_bill_to_group` (two calls; failure shows a retry) | Add `p_group_id` to `create_bill` (one atomic call) | `create_bill` and `lib/bills.ts` are in another session's files; a failed attach is recoverable | Open |
| GR-7 | Moving a bill in when someone on it isn't a member | Refused: "Invite Sam to Tahoe trip first" | Auto-add them to the group | Nobody joins a group without opening its link | Open |
| GR-8 | Existing "I paid" marks on move-in | Become group payments (participant → payer, the marked amount, source `bill_mark`) and the marks are cleared, in one transaction, logged on both sides | Refuse bills with marks | User decision U-G1 | Kept (user) |
| GR-9 | Taking a bill out of a group | Host only; refused while payments made from its old marks are live (undo them first) | Allow, leaving those payments in the group | Otherwise a marked payment would count in the group and the bill would ask for it again | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-10 | Enforcing "no per-bill settling / only members on a group bill" | Triggers on `bill_participants` (refuse marks, non-member inserts, member deletes on group bills) | Re-create `mark_paid`, `settle_difference`, `join_bill`, `add_participant`, `remove_participant`, `leave_bill` with checks | Works whatever signature the Sent via Venmo spec ends up with; one place; no edits to another session's function bodies | Open |
| GR-11 | Participant rows the group creates | `added = false` (opening the group link is consent) | `added = true` | Keeps L1 meaningful and keeps the "added" meaning ("put there by the host") | Open |
| GR-12 | Group-only links in People | Co-members are linked with 0 shared bills; caption "In a group together" | Only link through shared bills | User decision U-G5 ("joining counts") | Open |
| GR-13 | Reading co-members' profiles | `profiles` policy widened to co-members (same columns: name, Venmo username) | Read names only through the snapshot | User decision U-G5; one policy | Open |
| GR-14 | Group name in the Venmo note | "SplitIt: <group name>" through `venmoNote([name])`; off switch `GROUP_VENMO_NOTE_INCLUDES_NAME` (on); `VENMO_NOTE_INCLUDES_ITEMS` off also removes it | A separate note builder | User decision U-G5; reuses the tested note cleaning, no edit to `lib/paylinks.ts` | Open |
| GR-15 | Payments | Either side records or undoes; the client uuid makes a retry a no-op; partial and overpaying allowed (overpaying asks twice); "I paid" records the amount opened | Only the sender records (SE-5) | Cash on trips; ADR 0021 contract O | Open |
| GR-16 | How people get into a group | Link, QR, Share…, From contacts only; no adding from People | Add from People (plan) | Nobody is in a group they didn't open; less surface for the MVP | Open |
| GR-17 | Leaving | Only with no history (C-G10) and not the last member; otherwise Hide; no removing others | Leave at a $0 balance (needs split math in SQL); remove others | Leaving never changes money (L3) | Open |
| GR-18 | Hide | Owner-only row; Home's "Show hidden groups (N)" toggle with Unhide | Archive for everyone | Same pattern as `bill_seen` | Open |
| GR-19 | Group bills on Home | Not in "Your bills"; `my_bills()` excludes them | Show them in both places | One home per thing; group screen lists them | Open |
| GR-20 | Balances on Home's group rows | Not in the MVP (name + people count) | One snapshot per group on Home | Avoids N reads and Home files another session owns | Open |
| GR-21 | Realtime on group screens | None; refetch on focus and after writes | A `group:<id>` broadcast topic | Smaller security surface; bill screens keep Realtime | Open (before-production) |
| GR-22 | "Balances changed since you paid" notice | Not in the MVP | Store each person's net at their last payment | The new suggestion and Activity already show it | Open |
| GR-23 | Rename and new link | Any member; logged | Creator only | No special creator role to maintain when the creator leaves | Open |
| GR-24 | The group read | One `group_snapshot` jsonb function; strict parse, fail closed | Several table reads with exact counts | One snapshot of the data; can't be cut by `max_rows` (verify, criterion 37) | Open |
| GR-25 | Bill link of a group bill opened by a non-member | Refused (`in_group`): "This bill is in a group. Ask for the group's link." | Join the group automatically | A bill link wasn't consent to see the whole trip | Open |
| GR-26 | Account deletion with group history | Group money rows reference members with "restrict", so deletion fails until designed | Cascade (rewrites others' balances); set null (breaks the math) | Never change other people's balances silently | Open. **Privacy: needs your look** (before-production) |
| GR-27 | Expense date | Its created time; no date picker | A date field | Fewer fields; trips are logged as they happen | Open |
| GR-28 | Group invite message | "Join our group on SplitIt: <link>" (no group name) | Include the name | Same rule as PI-15: no names in messages | Open |
| GR-29 | Resuming a group invite after sign-in | Stored as `g-<code>` in the existing pending-invite slot, resumes to `/g/<code>` | A second storage key | No change to the root layout's single resume effect | Open |
| GR-30 | How a bill is named inside a group | "<Host>'s receipt" / "Your receipt" | Add a bill title column | Bills have no title today; out of scope | Open |
| GR-A1 | Who can change someone else's claim on a group bill | Any member can assign, reassign or remove any member's claim; every change logged; the assignee can change it back | Only the assigner or the assignee (needs `assigned_by` per claim) | Fits "A remembers what B got"; one rule; nothing stored per claim | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-A2 | Telling the assignee | Bill Activity line "Ana assigned Tacos to you"; no Home notice in the MVP | A Home card "Ana added 2 items for you" | Group bills aren't on Home and Home is another session's; an easy follow-up | Open |
| GR-A3 | Assigned claims and "fully claimed" | Count exactly like own claims | Need the assignee to confirm | Finishing a receipt is the point; it's visible and reversible | Open |
| GR-A4 | Assignments and payments | No link: a change after payments just moves balances and a new suggestion appears | Lock items once someone paid | Same as any edit after paying in a ledger | Open |
| GR-A5 | The write path | `assign_item(row, parts, expected)` security definer: whole claimer map with a stale-state token (`claims_changed`) | A per-person `set_claim_for` | One call per tap with the map shown; covers parts too | Open |
| GR-A6 | Who Activity says did it | The claims trigger uses the caller (`auth.uid()`) as actor; `assigned` / `unassigned` when the caller isn't the claim's person | Log from `assign_item` only | One place sees every claim write (as today) | Open |
| GR-A7 | The UI | "Who had this?" member chips in the item sheet on group bills; one write per tap; the parts stepper open to any member on group bills | A separate assign screen | Fewest elements, where people already look at an item | Open |
| GR-A8 | `set_item_split` | Unchanged (claimers only, every bill) | Open it to members on group bills | Group bills use `assign_item` for parts; no change to a shared function | Open |
| GR-A9 | The CLAUDE.md convention | Proposed wording in the spec (T19); the user or retro applies it | Edit CLAUDE.md now | Agents don't change CLAUDE.md | Open |

Amendment A1 (2026-11-26, from the staff-engineer review `docs/specs/2026-11-26-groups-mvp-eng.md`; all B and S items adopted, plus N1–N4, N6, N7, N9; N8 kept in scope):

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-S1 | A counted receipt that becomes incomplete; moving in incomplete bills with marks | The receipt drops out of balances and Settle shows "Balances don't include Hana's receipt yet: …"; `attach_bill_to_group` refuses a bill with marks that is incomplete or all-$0 (`not_complete`) | Keep counting the bill's last complete state (needs stored snapshots); allow the move | Keeps U-G3 exact and explains the swing; marks never become payments for a bill that doesn't count | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-S2 | Hiding tiny suggestions (refines GR-3) | Hide a payment only if it's ≤ 1¢ **and** both people's net is within 1¢; one `isSettledUp` predicate for every screen | Hide every ≤ 1¢ payment (could leave one person several cents short, told "settled up") | Nobody is ever more than 1¢ off while told "settled up" | Open. **Money: needs your look** |
| GR-S11 | Recording group payments vs Sent via Venmo | Groups keep "Did you pay Dee?" → I paid (contract O), no auto-record on open. `source` means how the user recorded it in both specs: `venmo` = recorded after a Venmo link the app opened, `manual` = Record a payment / Got paid, `bill_mark` = converted mark | Adopt SV's write-first auto-record in groups | Smallest change for the MVP; one meaning for one word across specs. Note for splitit-app-92 | Superseded by GR-P1 (user, 2026-11-26): groups record on open like Sent via Venmo; `source` and `method` are separate axes |
| GR-N3 | $0 equal shares and leaving | Shares with cents = 0 don't count as history for leaving | Count them (the "$0 trap") | A $0.01 split among 3 shouldn't trap two people | Open |
| GR-N5 | Group links and `add_participant` | Logged only: co-members are linked (L1), so a host can add a co-member to any non-group bill without them opening a link | Keep group links out of `add_participant` | Follows the user's "joining a group counts"; flagged as a consequence | Open |
| GR-T8-1 | A typed $0 in Exact amounts | Refused with "Leave it blank if they don't owe anything"; blank people are left out of the payload; T15 shows the message | Treat $0 as blank | T8 already built it; a $0 row would be stored as a meaningless share | Open |
| GR-T8-6 | Trimming titles and group names | Both sides trim exactly space, tab, newline, CR, VT, FF (`normalizeTitle` in lib; the same class in SQL); shared rows added for tab/newline-only titles | JS `trim()` vs SQL `btrim` (they differ) | Same rule both sides (CLAUDE.md) | Open |
| GR-P1 | How group payments are recorded (user decision U-G7, 2026-11-26; supersedes GR-S11) | Same as Sent via Venmo: opening the Venmo Pay link records it ("Sent via Venmo"); "Did you send…? → Yes" on desktop web or when the record's outcome is unknown; Undo | Contract O ("Did you pay? → I paid") | User decision; one way of paying across bills and groups | Kept (user) |
| GR-P1a | Payment axes | `method` (venmo/cash/other, SV's `private.is_payment_method`) = how the money moved; `source` (venmo_link/manual/bill_mark) = how the record was made; allowed pairs fixed (venmo_link → venmo only) | One combined field | splitit-app-92 confirmed `settled_method` means "how sent"; keeps the two questions separate | Open |
| GR-P2 | Check after an automatic group record | Inline "Did your $X to Dee go through?" once per payment on return; **no local notification** for group payments in the MVP | Reuse SV's 60 s notification | SV's notification data and handler are bill-shaped (billId, payerId); adding a group kind is a change to 92's module; easy follow-up | Open |
| GR-P3 | Recipient's undo | "Not received" (inline confirm), which undoes the payment and shows the sender "Dee hasn't received your $X…" | Plain Undo for both sides (GR-15) | Same words and meaning as SV for the person owed | Open |
| GR-T9-7 | `edit_expense` arguments | No `p_group_id` (the expense row has it), as T9's wrapper already assumes; T3 matches | Add `p_group_id` and check it | Fewer arguments; the group can't disagree with the row | Open |
| GR-T2-9 | Group name in the Venmo note | `cleanNoteName` in lib/groupSettleView.ts strips `\p{Cc}`, collapses whitespace, trims; not reusing lib/sentCheck.ts's private strip (it also removes `\p{Cf}`, breaking emoji ZWJ) | Export and reuse sentCheck's function | Keeps emoji intact in a note; sentCheck.ts is 92's file | Open |
| GR-T9-2 | Client ids on Hermes | `crypto.randomUUID` → `getRandomValues` → `Math.random` fallback for now; before-production item to use a proven source | Add `expo-crypto` now | Works today; collision risk is tiny but real with `Math.random` | Open (before-production) |
| GR-T8-2 | Expense amount cap in the form | Form stays at $99,999.99 (`lib/money.ts`) while the DB allows $999,999.99; raising it later | Raise `MAX_INPUT_CENTS` now | Stricter form is safe; `lib/money.ts` is shared | Open (before-production) |
- Decided by the user (2026-11-26): on bills that belong to a group, any group member can assign items (or shares of them) to other members — "on a friend trip person A will remember what person B got". This departs from the CLAUDE.md convention "users write only their own claims" for group bills only; non-group bills keep today's rule. To be recorded in ADR 0022, with a proposed CLAUDE.md wording update. People can join groups.
- GR-M4 (main session, 2026-11-26): Home places the Groups block after "Owed to you" and before "Settled" (Home v2 by splitit-app-96). Alternatives: top of Home; its own tab. Why: money you owe/are owed stays first; groups are next most active. Open.

T1 (G0 spike, implementer, 2026-11-26): decisions made building `lib/groupBalance.ts` and `lib/simplify.ts`.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-T1-1 | A broken input to `groupBalances` (payer, participant, share user or payment party who isn't a member; duplicate member, bill or share user; a self-payment; expense shares that don't add up to the amount; `totalCents` ≠ `split.totalCents`) | The whole call throws RangeError | C-G1(3) reads as "that bill doesn't count" | Fail closed, never a partial balance (C-G11). Under the invariants these can't happen, and T9's parser should already return `network` for them | Open |
| GR-T1-2 | Input shape | Bills and expenses carry an `id` (the spec lists `{ payerId, totalCents, split }`) | Use the array index | `counting` and `waiting` return bill ids | Open |
| GR-T1-3 | What `reason: 'all_zero'` covers | Any fully claimed bill with `unclaimedCents ≠ 0`: every item $0 with a total above 0 (proportional), and also a bill with no rows, or only discount rows, and a total above 0 (either mode). A bill with no rows and a $0 total counts and adds nothing | A third reason for "no rows" | That's exactly C-G1's rule (2); T10's copy ("every item is $0") also fits a bill with no items | Open |
| GR-T1-4 | Ranges `groupBalances` checks | Safe integers everywhere; expense amount ≥ 1, share cents ≥ 0, payment cents ≥ 1, bill total ≥ 0; sums checked with `Number.isSafeInteger`. No 99,999,999 upper bound | Re-check the C-G6/C-G7 upper bounds | The parser (T9) owns the field ranges; the math only needs integers that can't overflow | Open |
| GR-T1-5 | `simplify` API | `simplify(nets) → { full, shown }`; throws RangeError when a net isn't a safe integer or the nets don't sum to 0. Also exported: `netsOf(balances)` | Return only the shown list | The view needs `full` for the leftover bound and "Who paid what"; a non-zero sum would otherwise end with someone unsettled | Open |
| GR-T1-6 | Spec ambiguity in criterion 6: "{Ana +1, Ben +1, Cy −2} shows nothing and each leftover is ≤ 1¢" | Tested as each hidden payment ≤ 1¢ plus the C-G3 step-4 bound per person. Cy's leftover is 2¢ (two hidden 1¢ payments), which the bound allows | Read it literally (fails for Cy) | C-G3 step 4 is the precise rule; the PM may want to reword criterion 6 | Open (for the PM / T0) |
| GR-T1-7 | Output order | `members` in input order (the snapshot's joined_at order); `counting` and `waiting` in input bill order | Sort by id | The view lists them in snapshot order; nets and suggestions don't depend on order (tested by shuffling) | Open |

T8 (expense form logic, implementer, 2026-11-26): decisions made building `lib/groupExpense.ts`.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-T8-1 | A $0 Exact amount typed into a field | Refused (X5 `invalid`, copy "Leave it blank if they don't owe anything."); only blank fields are left out of the payload | Treat a typed $0.00 as blank and drop it silently | The spec says both "the form leaves $0 people out" and "the validator refuses a 0"; the X5 row is explicit, so the form shows the hint and the user clears the field. T15 may instead map a typed 0 to blank before calling the lib | Open (for the PM / T15) |
| GR-T8-2 | Text parsing of the amount fields | Not in T8: the lib works on cents; the screen parses. `parseMoneyInput` (lib/money.ts) caps at $99,999.99 (`MAX_INPUT_CENTS`), below C-G6's $999,999.99 | A second parser with the 99,999,999 cap in `lib/groupExpense.ts` | No copy of the money parser (lesson 2026-11-25). Consequence: with `parseMoneyInput`, T15 can't enter amounts above $99,999.99 (stricter than the DB, never looser). Raising it means a parameter on `parseMoneyInput` (shared file) | Open |
| GR-T8-3 | Off by copy | "Off by $0.01" for both over and under (criterion 71's text); the signed cents stay in the result | "$0.01 over" / "$0.01 short" | Matches the spec's literal copy; T15 can add the direction from the sign | Open |
| GR-T8-4 | Non-member and duplicate checks in the lib | `validateExpense` takes `memberIds` and refuses a non-member payer or share user (`not_member`) and a person listed twice (equal: `split.duplicate`; exact: `share.duplicate`, even if one entry is blank) | X8 "n/a" in the lib | Costs nothing and fails closed; the DB still decides | Open |
| GR-T8-5 | Payload shape | `expensePayload(draft)` returns the RPC arguments `{ p_title (trimmed), p_payer_id, p_amount_cents, p_method, p_shares }` or null when invalid; keys of `p_shares` in user-id order. `sharesFor(payload)` gives the rows the DB stores (equal via `equalPreview`) | Return only `p_shares` | T9 spreads it into `add_expense` / `edit_expense` with `p_id`, `p_group_id`, `p_expected_version` | Open |
| GR-T8-6 | Trimming the title | JS `trim()` (all Unicode whitespace) and the trimmed title is what's sent; SQL `btrim` trims only spaces by default | Trim only spaces in JS | Sending the trimmed title makes both sides agree on every title the app sends; a direct API call with a tab-only title would pass the DB (`char_length(btrim('\t')) = 1`). T3 may want `btrim(title, E' \t\n\r')` or a regex trim | Open (for T3) |
| GR-T8-7 | Id order | Sorted by the lowercase id in code-unit order (`sortUserIds`), so the lib matches SQL `collate "C"` on `uuid::text` even if an id arrived uppercase | Sort the strings as given | Same order as the DB whatever the casing | Open |
| GR-T8-8 | Exact with nobody filled in | `exactCheck` → `off_by` −amount ("Off by $300.00"); `validateExpense` → `split.no_people` | `invalid` | Not "adds up" either way; the off-by line tells the user how much is left to assign | Open |
| GR-T8-9 | Title trimming (A1.12 follow-up to GR-T8-6) | `normalizeTitle(s)` trims only `[ \t\n\r\v\f]` from both ends and is used by `validateExpense` and `expensePayload`; exported for group names (C-G8, T2/T9). Unicode spaces (U+00A0, U+2003, U+3000, U+FEFF) are kept and count as code points, so a title of only a non-breaking space is valid on both sides | Keep JS `trim()` | Same six characters as the DB's POSIX `[[:space:]]` trim, so lib and DB count and store the same title (criterion 113) | Open |

T1 follow-ups from Amendment A1 (implementer, 2026-11-26):

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-T1-6 | Criterion 6 ambiguity (logged above) | **Superseded by A1 S2**: criterion 6 and C-G3 were reworded; the two-sided hide rule is built and tested as amended | — | — | Closed |
| GR-T1-8 | Which net the hide rule reads | Each person's **input** net (before any suggestion), through `isSettledUp` | The running net at the moment the payment is chosen | That's how C-G3 step 3 reads ("both people's \|net\|"); it guarantees every leftover ≤ `GROUP_IGNORE_CENTS` (property-tested) because greedy keeps each person on one side | Open |
| GR-T1-9 | `isSettledUp` on a non-integer | Returns false | Throw RangeError | It's a display predicate; a bad value is never shown as "settled up". `groupBalances` and `simplify` already throw on bad values | Open |
| GR-T1-10 | `unclaimedRowIds` | Input row order (the snapshot's `group_position, unit_index` order), each id once, `[]` for `all_zero` bills | Sorted by id | T10 names the first rows as the bill shows them | Open |
| GR-T1-11 | S6 backstop test | `jest.spyOn` on `computeShares` returning shares that don't add up (the only way to reach the Σ-net check, since every earlier check would refuse a corrupted expense first); the corrupted-expense case from A1.5 is tested too | Only the corrupted-expense case | That case is refused by the earlier shares-sum check, so on its own it wouldn't show the backstop works | Open |

T2 groups core (supabase-engineer, 2026-11-26; `supabase/migrations/20261126082511_groups.sql`):

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-T2-1 | How the DB trims group names (A1.12) | `private.group_name(text)`: `regexp_replace(name, '^[ \t\n\r\v\f]+\|[ \t\n\r\v\f]+$', '', 'g')` in a standard string, plus a `groups_name_trimmed` check with the same pattern | POSIX `[[:space:]]` as A1.12 first suggested | Checked on the local Postgres 17.12 (`en_US.UTF-8`): `[[:space:]]` also strips U+00A0, U+3000, U+0085 and U+2028, which `normalizeTitle` keeps. The explicit class strips exactly the six. T3 should call `private.group_name` for expense titles too (it's generic despite the name) | Open |
| GR-T2-2 | `leave_group` in T2 | Built now with the lock order of B2(d) and one refusal, "the only member" (`cannot_leave`). T3–T5 re-create it to add the C-G10 history checks (and T5 the bill locks and participant deletes). Until then, any T3–T5 row that references `group_members` with `on delete restrict` makes the delete fail with 23503, which is answered as `cannot_leave` (tested with a stand-in table) | Leave `leave_group` to T5 as the spec body says | The coordinator asked for it in T2. The 23503 mapping is a backstop, so leaving can't silently drop history even before the checks exist | Open |
| GR-T2-3 | `leave_group` called again after leaving | 42501 `not_allowed`, same as any non-member | No-op success | A non-member gets one answer (C-G9). The screen navigates away after a successful leave, so a retry is unlikely. T9 can treat `not_allowed` after a leave as done | Open |
| GR-T2-4 | `group_events_kind_known` list | Includes T3–T5's kinds now (`expense_added`, `expense_edited`, `expense_deleted`, `payment_recorded`, `payment_undone`, `bill_added`, `bill_removed`) besides T2's five | Each task widens the constraint | One list, no constraint rewrites in T3–T5. Nothing can write those kinds until their functions exist: only security-definer functions insert events | Open |
| GR-T2-5 | `groups.created_by` on account deletion | Nullable, `on delete set null` | `restrict` | A creator who has left (no history) can still delete their account. Membership itself is `restrict` (S8) | Open |
| GR-T2-6 | `create_group` retry and clash rules (S3) | Same id, same caller and same trimmed name: returns the id, no new row or event. Same id with another name or from another caller: 22023 `id_conflict`. A null id: 22023 `invalid_id` (not a `GroupError`, so the app sees `network`; only a bug sends it) | Compare the name only | A different caller reusing an id is a clash, not a retry. If the creator renames the group and then retries create (rare), they get `id_conflict` | Open |
| GR-T2-7 | `group_created` details | `{ name }` (the name at creation) | `{}` | Activity can say "Ana created Tahoe trip" with the original name after a rename | Open |
| GR-T2-8 | Order of checks | Signed out → 42501; not a member (or no such group) → 42501 `not_allowed`; only then bad arguments (`invalid_name`, `invalid_hidden`). `join_group`: format → `invalid_code` before any lock; a current member re-joining a full group succeeds (membership is checked before the cap) | Validate arguments first | Same answer for every "can't see it" case, so nothing about other groups leaks. A re-join is a no-op, not a new seat | Open |
| GR-T2-9 | Control characters in group names | Not filtered (only the six edge characters are trimmed; inner tabs and newlines and other control characters are stored) | Refuse control characters | C-G8 doesn't ask for it. Names go into Venmo notes (U-G5), so T10 should strip control characters before building the note (`lib/paylinks.ts` has no such step today, checked by grep; `lib/sentCheck.ts` has a `[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}\p{Cs}]` strip that could be reused; lesson 2026-11-25 "control characters got through") | Open |
| GR-T2-10 | Applying the migration | Another session's `supabase migration up` applied this file (and `20261126080844_sent_via_venmo.sql`) while T2 was being tested; the applied text was checked to match the file on disk (one in-body comment edit was reverted to keep them identical). Later fixes go in new migrations | Edit the applied file | Migrations are append-only once applied anywhere | Closed |

T3 expenses (supabase-engineer, 2026-11-26; `supabase/migrations/20261126085945_group_expenses.sql`):

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-T3-1 | $0 equal shares and leaving (N3) vs the restrict FK on `expense_shares (group_id, user_id)` | `leave_group` deletes the leaver's 0-cent share rows (live and deleted expenses) just before deleting the membership | Keep the rows and drop the restrict FK for shares; refuse to leave | The FK would otherwise refuse the leave. No money changes: the rows are 0 and, with amount < n, the 0-cent users are the highest ids, so re-allocating among the rest gives every other share unchanged. Consequence: reloading that expense shows one person fewer in "who's in" | Open |
| GR-T3-2 | `add_expense` retry match | Same id + same group, same creator (caller), trimmed title, payer, amount, method and the stored shares map (current state, after any edits) → success; anything else → `id_conflict`. A deleted expense's id with the same content → success, stays deleted (A1.6) | Compare only the amount | A different member reusing an id is a clash, not a retry (same rule as GR-T2-6). Rare case: an add retried after someone edited it gets `id_conflict` | Open |
| GR-T3-3 | Event details | `expense_added` and `expense_deleted` carry `{expense_id, title, amount_cents}` (spec: without `title`); `expense_edited` exactly as spec | Spec shape only | The snapshot omits deleted expenses, so Activity couldn't name them otherwise ("Ana deleted Airbnb") | Open (for T10) |
| GR-T3-4 | Deleted expenses and versions | Delete doesn't bump `version`; a repeat delete is a no-op whatever the version; `edit_expense` on a deleted expense → `version_changed` (even with identical fields) | Bump version on delete; 42501 for a deleted expense | The screen's "someone changed this, reload" path already handles it; the refetched snapshot no longer has it | Open |
| GR-T3-5 | Check order | Signed out → 42501; not a member / no such expense / not creator or payer → 42501 `not_allowed` (one answer); `p_id` null → `invalid_id`; title/amount/method → `invalid_expense`; payer → `not_member`; shares shape → `invalid_shares`; share users → `not_member`; exact sum → `invalid_shares`; then (edit) deleted → `version_changed`, same fields → success, stale version → `version_changed` | Validate before authorizing | Same "can't see it" answer as T2 (GR-T2-8); A1.6 retry before the version check | Open |
| GR-T3-6 | `p_shares` keys and values | Keys must be lowercase canonical uuids (an uppercase key → `invalid_shares`); values must be JSON integers (`1.5`, `1.0` and strings like `"100"` are refused) via `private.jsonb_int_between`; equal capped at 50 keys, exact uncapped (the member cap limits it) | Accept any uuid casing | Lowercase keys can't collide after casting, and the C-G4 sort assumes them; lib always sends lowercase. JSON duplicate keys can't be seen (jsonb keeps the last); lib never sends one | Open |
| GR-T3-7 | `private.allocate` arithmetic | numeric (exact integers) instead of bigint for `amount * weight`; same results, no overflow path; output cast to integer. Mirrors lib also for a negative amount, `allocate(0, [])` and `allocate(0, [0,0])` | bigint as the spec says | Identical answers on every row; nothing to overflow | Open |
| GR-T3-8 | Amount argument type | `p_amount_cents bigint` (column stays integer 1..99,999,999) | integer | An oversized number reaches the range check and returns `invalid_expense`, not a cast error the app would show as `network` | Open |
| GR-T3-9 | `deleted_by` integrity | Check constraint `deleted_by in (created_by, payer_id)` instead of an FK | FK to `group_members` | Both are already restrict-referenced; no extra lock on delete | Open |

T9 (data layer, implementer, 2026-11-26): decisions made building `lib/groups.ts`, `lib/groupInviteLink.ts` and the `g-` invite in `lib/pendingInvite.ts`. Written against the spec's contracts; checked by eye against `20261126082511_groups.sql` (T2) once it landed (names, arguments and messages match). T12 reconciles the rest live.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-T9-1 | Telling a group invite from a bill code | Stored as `g-<code>`; `normalizeInviteCode` now refuses any value starting with `g-` (any case), and `postSignInRoute` checks the prefix first (`g-` + 20 hex → `/g/<code>`, any other `g-…` → `/`). Group codes are trimmed and lowercased | Leave `normalizeInviteCode` alone (a `/b/g-…` link would then be stored as a bill code and resume to `/g/…`) | Bill codes are 20 hex in the DB (`bills_join_code_format`), so no real one starts with `g-`; the two kinds can never be mixed up. Until T13 adds `app/g/[code].tsx`, a resumed group invite lands on the not-found screen | Open |
| GR-T9-2 | Client ids for `create_group` / `add_expense` / `record_payment` | `newClientId()`: `crypto.randomUUID`, else `crypto.getRandomValues`, else `Math.random` (lowercase v4). `createIntentId()` gives the same id for the same intent key until `done()` is called after a success | Add `expo-crypto` | No new dependency in a data-layer task. The id is an idempotency key, not a secret: a guessed id can only make a write fail with `id_conflict`. **Verify** which source the Hermes native build has (before-production item to add) | Open |
| GR-T9-3 | The snapshot parser's last check | After parsing, the snapshot is run once through `groupBalances(balanceInputOf(...))`; a RangeError makes the read `network`. `balanceInputOf` is exported for T10 | Parse only | C-G11 says "never a partial balance": anything the math refuses is caught at the read, so screens never see a snapshot they can't add up | Open |
| GR-T9-4 | How strict the snapshot parser is | Also refuses: a claimed row that carries a name (A1.7 says null), an unclaimed row without one, an expense creator who isn't a member (restrict FK), an exact share of $0, more than 50 equal shares, a row id on two bills. Allows: `group.created_by` null (GR-T2-5) or a non-member (they left), a payment `bill_id` for a bill outside the snapshot (detached or deleted) | Check only the A1.7 / S6 list | Each is an invariant of the T2–T7 schema; a breach means the read is broken. T12 will show if one is too strict | Open |
| GR-T9-5 | Errors for a malformed id argument | A non-uuid group, bill, row, expense or payment id → `not_allowed` with no request (there is no `not_found` in `GroupError`; it's what the DB answers for "can't see it"). A malformed client id → `network` (only a bug sends one) | Add `not_found` | Keeps the spec's union; same answer as the server | Open |
| GR-T9-6 | Backstop mappings | 23503 → `cannot_leave` for `leave_group` and → `claims_changed` for `assign_item` (the DB maps these itself, A1.2/A1.6); `in_group` accepted from `attach_bill_to_group` (T5 trigger) | Strictly `network` | If the DB mapping is ever missed, the screen still refetches or shows the right copy instead of "check your connection" | Open |
| GR-T9-7 | `edit_expense` arguments | `p_id, p_expected_version, p_title, p_payer_id, p_amount_cents, p_method, p_shares` (no `p_group_id`: an expense can't move groups) | Send `p_group_id` too | The spec says "same fields"; T3 must match this or T12 flags it | Open (T3) |
| GR-T9-8 | Read guards | `my_groups()` with 1,000 rows or more → `network` (could be cut by `max_rows`); `fetchGroupEvents` rows out of `seq` order, a repeated id or more than 100 → `network`; `fetchBillGroup` for a bill or group RLS hides → `not_allowed` | Trust the order and size | Lesson 2026-11-25 (max_rows cuts without an error); fail closed | Open |
| GR-T9-9 | Local argument checks | Wrappers refuse, with no request, what the DB would refuse anyway: names/titles by C-G8/C-G6 after `normalizeTitle`, amounts by C-G6/C-G7, equal shares other than 1, exact shares outside 1..99,999,999, `from = to` and `source = 'bill_mark'` (`invalid_payment`), parts outside 1..20 (`invalid_parts`). Membership and Σ = amount are left to the DB | Send everything | No round trip for a value that can only fail; the DB still decides | Open |
- GR-M5 (main session, 2026-11-26): group bills have exactly one payer in the MVP; split-paying (splitit-app-96's who-paid, X3) is refused on group bills (its set_payers refuses several payers when bills.group_id is set; attach refuses a split-paid bill). Anyone else who paid adds a simple group expense. Alternative: build group balances on bill_payers (paid per person) — a small lib change later. Why: smallest MVP; who-paid lands after Groups. Its 4a ports our mark triggers and attach's mark conversion onto bill_marks. Open.
- Decided by the user (2026-11-26): group payments are recorded like regular bills (Sent via Venmo): opening the Venmo link records the payment, with the "Did you send…? → Yes" fallback and Undo; methods from lib/paymentMethods.ts. Supersedes GR-S11's "Did you pay? → I paid".

T11 (small lib edits, implementer, 2026-11-26): `lib/activityText.ts` (group-bill sentences), `lib/people.ts` (group-only links), new `lib/itemAssign.ts`. Written against C-G12 and T5's event shapes; T7 and T5 must log exactly these details or the sentences are skipped.

| ID | Decision | Picked | Alternatives | Why | Status |

| GR-T11-2 | Quotes in the converted-marks clause | `'I paid'` in single quotes: "Ana moved this bill into Tahoe trip. 2 'I paid' marks became group payments." | Double quotes as written in the spec's prose | Same screen already reads "cleared 1 'I paid' mark" and "undid your 'I paid'"; one style per screen. 0 converted → no clause | Open |
| GR-T11-3 | Event shapes accepted | `assigned`/`unassigned`: `{ user_id, unit? }` (unit like `claimed`'s), item name required; `moved_to_group`: `{ group_id, converted ≥ 0 }` both required; `removed_from_group`: `{ group_id }`. An assigned/unassigned event whose `user_id` is the actor is malformed (null; C-G12 logs claimed/unclaimed for the caller) | Render self-assignment as "claimed" | Fail closed like `paid_difference`. T7 must emit `unit` only in claims-trigger form `[index, size]` if at all | Open (T7) |
| GR-T11-4 | `people_i_split_with()` rows with `shared_bills = 0` | Parsed as valid (was malformed; 0 = linked only through a group, T5's `people_i_split_with`). Caption "In a group together" (no date), a11y "Sam, in a group together"; the same-name handle suffix still applies | Hide the date only | Criterion 58; the date of a group join isn't a "bill" date | Open |
| GR-T11-5 | `toggleAssignee` on a broken map | Throws RangeError for parts outside 1..20 (or an empty id), even when removing the bad entry | Clamp to 1..20 | Clamping would silently change someone's parts in the map sent to `assign_item`; the bill parser never produces one | Open |
| GR-T11-6 | Chip order and labels | Viewer first ("You"), then name ignoring case, ties by id; each member once; claim keys that aren't members get no chip. Labels "Ben, had this" / "Ben, didn't have this", and "You had this" / "You didn't have this" for the viewer | "You, had this" | Reads naturally for the viewer; T18 uses `a11yLabel` as is | Open |
